Four review threads on PR 3879 are still open (Jeremy's lazy-pack question and Ben's mask_t, two-control-flow-paths and syscall-comment threads); for each, and for Ben's review summary and Wade's PR-level evaluation, this page gives the state of the code at head 524c510609 and a reply jhan can post. All four are settled: mask_t and the syscall comment by the code, the two-paths thread by Ben himself in the 2026-09-09 walkthrough ("no, that's fine, I'm okay with this"), and the lazy-pack thread by the 2026-08-31 measurements once one number is corrected (the posted bound used the wrong pack cost and is 3.2-4.7 % of a decode step's CPU time, est., not 0.5-0.8 %; the conclusion "small" survives). Words used here

tron, runtron
tron is the inference program under test; runtron is its command-line tool.
PR 3879 (this PR), PR 0, PR 0b, PR 2
The original PR 3879 was split on 2026-09-08. PR 0 = #4267 (page-share counters, instrumentation; merged 2026-09-10). PR 0b = #4265 (a one-line memory-order fix plus a test; merged 2026-09-10 17:45 UTC). This PR keeps the number 3879 and now holds only the canonical AMX path. PR 2 = the K mirror (a second copy of the attention keys), parked as a local branch, not opened.
old head, head
The old head 60d66d9c04 is the commit Ben, Jeremy and Wade reviewed. The head 26a0338c3b is the current tip of the PR; every line number on this page refers to it unless marked otherwise.
AMX, AVX-512
AMX (Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions) multiplies small matrices held in 8 tile registers. AVX-512 is the vector instruction set the existing attention code uses.
the dotter, the dotter loop
The existing AVX-512 attention code path: one query-key dot product per token, then the softmax step. Unchanged by this PR.
KV page, dense page
A KV page holds the keys and values of 64 tokens. A dense page, for one query, is a full 64-token page that the query may see in one visibility range and that lies inside its sliding window. Only dense pages take the AMX path.
kv_mul, GQA
Grouped-query attention lets several query heads share one KV head; kv_mul is that ratio. The kernels serve head size 128 with kv_mul 4, hence the namespace name amx_attn_h128g4.
bf16
bfloat16, the 2-byte floating-point format K, V and (for eligible executors) Q are stored in.
pack, packed Q, amx_qpack
A pack copies one token's 4 query heads (1024 bytes) into the 4096-byte tile-operand layout the QK kernel reads. amx_qpack is the buffer of such slots, one per token of the minibatch.
attn_accum
The per-worker scratchpad struct of software attention (running softmax state v*, s*, m* and a validity flag per token and head), one instance per pool worker in the attn vector, plus two per hardware-attention pass.
thread_local
A C++ storage class: one object per thread, created on first use and kept for the thread's lifetime.
range_hint, token_range, ranged_mask
The ranged_mask splits the KV tokens into ranges; within one token_range [tok_lo, tok_hi) every token has the same visibility answer for a given query. range_hint is the pointer to the range the code is currently in; the dotter loop advances it when it steps past tok_hi.
mask_t<N>
tron's fixed-width bit mask type (h/common/mask.hpp). Ben asked why the code did not use it.
begin_region / end_region
The bracket that loads the AMX tile configuration (LDTILECFG) and releases it (TILERELEASE) around one worker's page-range call.
Codex
The automated reviewer Jeremy ran; his two comments are signed "Codex review, submitted at Jeremy's request".
resolve
GitHub's per-thread "Resolve conversation" button. A review body and a PR-level comment have no such button; they are answered in a PR comment or in the re-review request.

1. Jeremy (Codex): Lazy tile region and Q pack

Open   Action: Decision needed from jhan  |  open on GitHub

The structure the 2026-08-31 reply described is unchanged at 4290402491. One number in that reply must be corrected: its 0.5-0.8 % waste bound used the mirror build's 6.3 ns pack, while this PR's only packer costs 63 ns per pack, so the bound is 3.2-4.7 % of a decode step's CPU time (est.), about 0.3-0.4 % of wall-clock decode. Then choose: keep the eager order (a) or adopt the lazy pack (b), sketched below.

The comment

Jeremy (Codex review), 2026-08-25, comment 3858305362, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1367 (now :1342 at 4290402491)

amx_on only checks geometry and CPU support, so every eligible page range executes LDTILECFG/TILERELEASE and packs each query before the dense-page predicate is evaluated. Partial pages, masked/sliding-window pages, and null-mirror fallbacks therefore pay AMX setup plus a 4 KB pack even though every page uses AVX. Could we lazily begin the region and pack Q only when the first page actually satisfies the dense predicate?

— Codex review, submitted at Jeremy’s request

jhan's earlier reply (already posted)
jhan, 2026-08-31, comment 3896108314, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1367 (now :1342 at 4290402491)

You are right about the structure. begin_region() (the call that runs LDTILECFG, the instruction that loads the AMX tile configuration) runs once for every page range with amx_on true (self_attention.hpp:1374). Each token's query group is packed at its first visible page, before any page has passed the dense-page test (the test that lets a full 64-token page use the AMX kernel; it lives in apply_page_tok, 1564-1566). So a page range with no dense page pays the setup and then does all its work in the AVX fallback loop.

Three details changed after you filed this: - The gate now also requires the executor to store activations as bf16, not only geometry and CPU support (02208bebcf). - A pack now writes about 1 KB of live data into the 4 KB buffer. The other 3 KB is padding, zeroed once per thread rather than on every pack (35b3f3caef). - Pages that are fully masked, or fully outside the sliding window, are skipped before any pack (1394/1397). A missing mirror with amx_on true can only happen when the arena's no-throw allocation failed, which is rare. The common fallback cases are partial pages and window-clipped pages, as you said.

We measured the question on the production CPU (Xeon 6962P; results recorded under exec/results/jeremy-measure-20260831 in our campaign repo): - The LDTILECFG + TILERELEASE pair costs about 229 cycles, roughly 85 ns, paid once per page-range call. - One pack costs 6.3 ns in the mirror build (it is a 1 KB memcpy); the canonical build's scalar packer costs 63 ns. - For scale, one dense page's full QK + softmax + PV costs 686 ns (mirror kernel) to 1250 ns (canonical). - How often the setup is wasted, counted per call during qwen-3-4b decode: 21-24% of AMX-on page-range calls finish with zero AMX pages at 2K and 8K contexts, and 37% in a short run with a 32-token prompt. These are mostly sections holding only the partial tail page, as you predicted.

So the waste is frequent but small. Those calls burn roughly 26-32 microseconds of region setup per decode step, plus at most their share of the pack time, spread across 12 attention worker threads. The upper bound is about 0.5-0.8% of one decode step's CPU time, which is on the order of 0.05% of wall-clock decode. At that size we prefer keeping one uninterrupted tile region per call over threading lazy state through the per-token, per-page hot path, so the code stays as is. If a future model shape shifts the balance (a CPU-served sliding-window model, for example), the first move on record is a ~5-line change: pack only when the page is full, and open the region at the first pack.

What was asked

Jeremy's automated reviewer observed that every AMX-eligible page-range call loads the tile configuration and packs each query before any page has passed the dense-page test (the test that lets a full 64-token page use the AMX kernels). Calls whose pages all fall back to the AVX loop pay that setup for nothing. He asked whether the region could be opened, and the query packed, only when the first page is dense.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

The structure is unchanged at 4290402491: begin_region() runs once per apply_page_range call, before the page loop [self_attention.hpp:1342-1349]; end_region() now runs from a tron::finally scope guard (an object whose destructor runs the given code when the function exits) declared next to begin_region() [:1350-1354], which changes where the call is written, not when the region opens or closes; the pack runs at most once per token per call, at the token's first page that passes the outer visibility and window checks [:1389-1397]; is_dense_amx_page is evaluated after the pack, inside apply_page_tok [:1609-1611]. The dispatch test pins this order: a 63-token page packs once and calls no kernel [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:200-202, :152].

The posted numbers for the region pair (about 85 ns per call), the wasted-call share (21-24 % of AMX-on calls at prompt 2048 and 8192, 37 % at prompt 32) and the per-page kernel cost stand. The posted upper bound of 0.5-0.8 % does not: it used the 6.3 ns memcpy pack of the mirror build, while this PR's only packer, the scalar pack_q_group_128x4, was measured at 63 ns per pack [exec/results/jeremy-measure-20260831/bench-qpack.txt:8-10; same loop body at head, src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:102-116; not re-measured]. With 63 ns the same arithmetic gives 3.2 / 3.8 / 4.7 / 1.1 % of one decode step's CPU time for prompt 2048 with 1 user, prompt 2048 with 8 users, prompt 8192 with 8 users and prompt 32 with 1 user (est.; it assumes the calls that end with zero AMX pages pack as much per call as the average call) [scratchpad bound_recalc.py]. Spread over the 12 attention workers that is about 0.3-0.4 % of wall-clock decode, below the 0.6 % difference between the two same-code arms (clean build, AMX build with TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1) of the 2026-09-09 round [exec/results/qwen8u8k-pr1-half-20260909T1704.txt]. So the correction changes the number, not the conclusion: the waste is real and small, and no A/B run can measure a change of this size.

Lazy pack: what would change (option b, not applied)

Jeremy's suggestion has two steps. Step 1 packs a token's query group only when the page in hand is dense, instead of at the first visible page. Step 2 opens the tile region (the LDTILECFG instruction that loads the tile configuration) at the first pack instead of before the page loop. At 4290402491 the region is closed by a tron::finally scope guard (a small object whose destructor runs the given code when the function exits) declared next to begin_region() [self_attention.hpp:1350-1354], so step 2 sets a flag at the first pack and makes the guard call end_region() only when the flag is set. Every value the dense predicate needs is already in scope at the pack site in apply_page_range: the page, its hint range, the query and first-key positions, the window size, and the query's job index [:1361-1397]. The EAGLE rule for begin (EAGLE = a speculative-decoding scheme whose draft models skip the first key token; the rule is at :1582-1590 in apply_page_tok) is the only piece that has to be written a second time.

h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp at 4290402491, apply_page_range, the pack site (:1389-1397):

           const bf16* amx_qp = nullptr;
 #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH
           if constexpr (amx_eligible) {
             if (amx_on) {
-              amx_qp = packed_amx_query<geometry>(
-                  amx_qpack[batch_job_ix].v, amx_packed, batch_job_ix, kv_head, q);
+              // Pack only for a page the AMX path will take: the same predicate
+              // apply_page_tok evaluates, on the same inputs (all in scope here).
+              const size_t begin = (enclosing_state.model_ref.plugin.is_eagle &&
+                                       k_position == 0 && q_position != 0)
+                                       ? 1
+                                       : 0;
+              const bool query_visible = run_visible_in_software
+                                             ? page_begin_hint->visible(tok_ix)
+                                             : page_begin_hint->sw_required(tok_ix);
+              if (is_dense_amx_page(page, begin, page.count(), query_visible,
+                      *page_begin_hint, q_position, k_position, sliding_window_size)) {
+                if (!amx_region_open) {  // step 2: open the tile region lazily
+                  amx_attn_h128g4::begin_region();
+                  amx_region_open = true;
+                }
+                amx_qp = packed_amx_query<geometry>(
+                    amx_qpack[batch_job_ix].v, amx_packed, batch_job_ix, kv_head, q);
+              }
             }
           }
 #endif

Step 2 only, the region block and its scope guard (:1342-1354):

       if (amx_on) {
         TRON_ASSERT_LE(items.size(), max_minibatch_size);
         ...
         amx_qpack.resize(items.size());
-        amx_attn_h128g4::begin_region();
       }
+      // Set at the call's first pack; the guard below reads it at exit.
+      bool amx_region_open = false;
       // Release the tile configuration on every exit from this function,
       // including an early return added later.
       tron::finally end_amx_region([&] {
-        if (amx_on) amx_attn_h128g4::end_region();
+        if (amx_region_open) amx_attn_h128g4::end_region();
       });
       (the flag is declared before the guard on purpose: the lambda captures
        by reference and reads the flag when the function exits)

-    REQUIRE(probe.pack_calls == (packs ? 1 : 0));            // :152

   (and the header comment :11-14, which describes the eager pack)

What else changes. The comment above the AMX block in apply_page_range [:1321-1327] says the pack happens "before that page's dense predicate" and that the tile configuration "is held for the whole call"; both sentences are rewritten. The PR description's commit-2 paragraph says the same in one clause ("done after the outer visibility and window checks and before the dense predicate") and gets the new order. The gate in apply_page_tok [:1609-1611] can stay as it is: it re-evaluates the predicate on dense pages only (five integer compares), which keeps apply_page_tok correct on its own; dropping the predicate there and trusting the caller is the alternative. Note [AMX attention dispatch] and doc/amx_software_attention.md do not describe the pack timing, so they need no edit.

Size. 21 added and 4 removed lines in self_attention.hpp (counted from the sketch above; the region block contributes 3 of the added and 2 of the removed), 2 changed lines plus a 4-line comment in the test, one comment block, one PR-body clause (computed from the sketch).

Expected effect. Calls that end with zero dense pages (21-24 % of AMX-on calls at prompt 2048 and 8192, 37 % at prompt 32, measured 2026-08-31) stop paying the pack (63 ns per token) and, with step 2, the region pair (about 85 ns). That is the 3.2-4.7 % of a decode step's CPU time above, about 0.3-0.4 % of wall-clock decode (est.), which the 0.6 % same-code spread hides in any A/B run. A debug counter of packs inside zero-AMX calls, before and after, is the measurement that would show the change did what it says.

Verification if adopted. Build with the option on and run t_amx_numerics, the updated t_amx_dispatch_dtype and t_llama_unit on delphi-3bda; build with the option off and run t_llama_unit; repeat the three-arm decode round (clean / kill switch / AMX; expect the same +17 % within noise). Then post the reply with this closing instead of the last paragraph:

The lazy pack is in: <commit>. A token's query group is packed, and the tile region opened, at the token's first page that passes `is_dense_amx_page`, so a call without a dense page pays neither the pack nor the tile configuration; the dispatch test's 63-token case now expects no pack. The saving is at most the 0.3-0.4 % of wall-clock decode above (est.) and is not measurable against the 0.6 % same-code spread; the three attention tests pass on the Xeon 6962P with the option on, and t_llama_unit with it off.

Decision for jhan

(1) Post the correction of the 0.5-0.8 % bound either way. (2) Choose (a) keep the eager order: post the reply as drafted, resolve; or (b) adopt the lazy pack: apply the sketch below, update the dispatch test, rebuild and run the three tests on delphi-3bda, push, then post the reply with its last paragraph replaced by the (b) closing given below.

Insufficient data

  • Packs made inside the calls that end with zero AMX pages were not counted separately (the 2026-08-31 counter counts every pack); the bound assumes they pack as much per call as the average call. A debug counter that sums packs only in zero-AMX calls would replace the assumption.
  • The 63 ns per pack figure was measured on the 2026-08-31 kernel source (commit 1d158249e2); the head's loop body is the same apart from types and constant names, but it was not re-measured.

Reply to post

The 2026-08-31 answer still describes the structure at 4290402491: `begin_region()` (the call that loads the AMX tile configuration; AMX = Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions) runs once per `apply_page_range` call (h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1348), each token's query group is packed at most once per call, at its first page that passes the visibility and window checks (:1393-1394), and the dense-page test runs after the pack, in `apply_page_tok` (:1609-1611).

One number in that answer needs a correction. Its 0.5-0.8 % bound used the 6.3 ns pack of the K-mirror build (a second copy of the keys in tile layout, not part of this PR); this PR's only packer is the scalar `pack_q_group_128x4`, measured at 63 ns per pack. The same arithmetic with 63 ns gives 3.2-4.7 % of one decode step's CPU time (est.; 1.1 % at a 32-token prompt), which is about 0.3-0.4 % of wall-clock decode spread over the 12 attention workers. That is below the 0.6 % difference between the two arms of the 2026-09-09 round that run the same AVX code (clean build, and AMX build with TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1), so an A/B run cannot resolve it.

I keep the eager order in this PR: the lazy pack is a small change (about 25 lines in one file), but its saving is below what we can measure, and one uninterrupted tile region per call keeps the code simpler. It stays the recorded first change if a per-call count of wasted packs, or a sliding-window model served on the CPU, shows a larger saving.

2. Ben: Packed-Q storage: thread_local vector, attn_accum, or array

Replied and resolved   Action: Nothing left to do  |  open on GitHub

Replied by jhan 2026-09-10 22:52 UTC (comment 3984199726) and resolved; the change landed as 752e9f571a (std::vector member of attn_accum). The text below is the draft the reply was based on; its line numbers are for 26a0338c3b.

Ben is right: the packed-Q buffer can live in attn_accum, and the reason in the head's comment ("per (token, kv_head)"; "this PR keeps attn_accum's ownership unchanged") was a scope statement, not a technical obstacle. Two local prototypes (vector member 5b5eeca67f = option B, array member 250ff8204d = option A) build and pass the three attention tests on delphi-3bda. Recommendation: B. The PR head 26a0338c3b still has the thread_local; jhan has not chosen a form. Detailed analysis: ~/workspace/intel-AMX/PR3879/new-PRs/PR1/reviews/amx_qpack-2-attn_accum.html. NOTE for posting: reply_text is written for the state AFTER option B is cherry-picked and pushed (it says the buffer is now a member); do not post it before that, and fill in the commit hash.

The comment

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3936949728, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1369 (old head 60d66d9c04)

If I'm not mistaken, this is going to result in a runtime allocation. Usually this is frowned upon but the facts that

  1. it's thread_local
  2. attention workers are a long-lived pool
  3. batch sizes are bounded

collectively mean that this allocation will be amortized over many operations, which makes it probably okay.

Still, I do wonder why we don't just handle this consistently with the other intermediates needed by attention and attach it to attn_accum. Moreover, we know max_minibatch_size statically so surely this should just be an array.

Ben, 2026-09-09, comment 3973410716, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1369 (old head 60d66d9c04)

I'm still not convinced that this couldn't (and, indeed, shouldn't) live in attn_accum. Let's figure out whether this is really the case.

What was asked

Ben looks at the thread_local std::vector that holds the packed queries (one 4096-byte slot per token of the minibatch, each slot the tile-layout copy of one token's four query heads) inside apply_page_range. He accepts that the runtime allocation is amortized (thread-local storage, long-lived worker pool, bounded batch), but asks (a) why the buffer is not attached to attn_accum (the per-worker scratchpad struct that already holds the attention intermediates v_stars, s_stars, m_stars, valid) like the other intermediates, and (b) why it is not a std::array, since max_minibatch_size is a compile-time constant. His 2026-09-09 follow-up, written after the current head 26a0338c3b was pushed, says he is still not convinced it could not and should not live in attn_accum and asks to settle the question.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

Current placement. The slot type amx_qpack_slot (alignas(64), Q_PACK_ELEMS_2048 bf16 = 4096 bytes) and the function-local thread_local std::vector<amx_qpack_slot> amx_qpack are declared inside apply_page_range under #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1324-1327; h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:118, :141]. The vector is resized to items.size() and the tile region opened only when amx_on is true [self_attention.hpp:1333-1339]. The comment gives two reasons: a pack is per (token, kv_head) and "this PR keeps attn_accum's ownership unchanged"; an array would reserve max_minibatch_size x 4096 bytes of thread-local storage per instantiation on every thread [self_attention.hpp:1316-1321]. The per-call bitset amx_packed records which tokens are packed in this call [self_attention.hpp:1332]. packed_amx_query packs a token at most once per call into the slot it is handed [self_attention.hpp:1377-1380, :1417-1430]. Ben's original comment anchors on the thread_local line of the old head [self_attention.hpp:1369 @ 60d66d9c04].

Ownership of attn[scratchpad]. apply_page_range takes one kv_head and one scratchpad index per call [self_attention.hpp:1264-1273]. It has one production call site: run_sections passes the attention worker index worker_ix as the scratchpad, once per section of that worker's ready or pending plan [self_attention.hpp:988-995]. By the convention of Note [Attention workers are the last pool workers], attention worker i runs on pool worker num_workers() - n_attn_workers + i, a fixed thread of the pool; the Note says per-worker scratchpads are keyed by attention worker index and two threads claiming one index would race [self_attention.hpp:798; h/tron/models/model.hpp:2287-2305; h/tron/threading.hpp:762-767]. Inside the call the token's running state is attn[scratchpad] [self_attention.hpp:1372-1373, :383-389]. Three phases touch attn. Before the sections phase, one worker clears the validity flags of every attn element inside the free_scratchpads barrier phase [self_attention.hpp:736-740, :758-761, :893-901]. In the sections phase each worker writes its own attn[worker_ix]. In the join phase, which a worker enters as soon as its own run_sections returns (no barrier between :825 and :834), the join target is attn[0]: after polling that every section of a kv_head is finished, a joining worker writes attn[0]'s v_stars, s_stars, m_stars and valid for that kv_head and calls attn[0].finish [self_attention.hpp:825, :834, :1060-1063, :1070, :1094-1095, :1129-1132, :1141, :1700-1750]. So attn[0] is written by several workers at once on disjoint kv_head slices while worker 0 may still be in a sections call; the draft's sentence "only that worker touches attn[scratchpad]" was too broad. The narrower claim holds: no phase other than the owning worker's own sections calls reads or writes a packed-Q slot, because join_page_ranges and finish touch only v_stars, s_stars, m_stars and valid. A slot stored in attn[scratchpad] and indexed by batch_job_ix (the token's position in the minibatch, like v_stars) is therefore touched by exactly one worker. The "per (token, kv_head)" reason does not block this: every call serves one kv_head and re-packs (the bitset is per call), so the slot content is scratch of one call under either placement [self_attention.hpp:1267, :1332, :1424-1428]. The unit tests call apply_page_range directly with scratchpad 0 and 1 from one thread, which works with either placement [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:1537-1539, :1600-1604; t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:149-150]. A grep over h/, src/, t/ and gen/ at 26a0338c3b finds amx_qpack and packed_amx_query only in self_attention.hpp (:1305, :1324, :1327, :1338, :1379-1380, :1417), so neither the join nor the hardware-attention path can name the buffer. The parallel page adds one nuance (its claim C1, cited to model.hpp:2043-2087, not re-verified here): the helper/attention split of the pool can move between passes, so a worker index and an OS thread coincide within a pass, not for the life of the process; this favours keying the buffer by worker index (a member) over keying it by thread (thread_local).

What attn_accum holds today, inline versus owned. attn_accum has v_stars (max_minibatch_size x btensor_t<scratch_query_width>), s_stars and m_stars (max_minibatch_size x scratch_query_heads floats each) and valid (max_minibatch_size x scratch_kv_heads bools) [self_attention.hpp:280-288]. scratch_query_width = max(n_heads x head_size, max_kernel_query_width), scratch_query_heads = max(n_heads, max_kernel_query_heads), scratch_kv_heads = max(n_kv_heads, max_kernel_kv_heads) [self_attention.hpp:232-237; model.hpp:723-728; h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:334-358]. For the tp1/tp2/tp4 executors btensor_t is hardware::btensor = dmatensor<bf16, N> [h/tron/models/common.hpp:73-75, :82-83, :90-92; h/tron/hardware/btensor.hpp:20-21]. dmatensor stores no payload inline: its only data member is a unique_dma handle, and its default constructor allocates output_size x 2 bytes of DMA memory (DMA = direct memory access; this pool is the memory the FPGA path reads and writes) (output_size rounds N up to a multiple of 4096 elements) and zero-fills it [h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:163, :176, :180-181; h/system/memory.hpp:177-192, :213-224]. So v_stars already allocates at construction: attn.resize(app_pool().num_workers()) performs max_minibatch_size DMA allocations per attn_accum [self_attention.hpp:472]. Computed from these constants for llama_3p1_8b (dim 4096, 32 heads, 8 KV heads, head size 128; kernel geometry {n_heads, {n_kv_heads, head_size}}; max_minibatch_size 128) [h/tron/plugins/llama.hpp:1564, :143-146, :184, :200-202]: inline 128 x 16 (v_stars handles) + 128 x 32 x 4 (s_stars) + 128 x 32 x 4 (m_stars) + 128 x 8 (valid) = 2,048 + 16,384 + 16,384 + 1,024 = 35,840 bytes, about 35 KiB; DMA memory owned through v_stars 128 x 8,192 = 1,048,576 bytes (1 MiB); footprint 1,084,416 bytes, about 1.03 MiB. These inline sizes (35,840 and 286,720 bytes) are computed from the declared types; the 16-byte handle and the 24-byte vector header come from a standalone replica compiled with g++ 11.4 on x86-64 libstdc++ (scratchpad/handle_sizes.cpp), and the parallel page's claim C3 reports that a standalone compile of the same layout reproduces both totals; no sizeof(attn_accum) was printed inside a tron build. For a generated plugin with the same shape and max_minibatch_size 1024 (the ingested llama-3.1-8b and qwen-3-4b headers, generated-header snapshot in gen/ dated 2026-08-27) [ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:264; gen/src/tron/h/tron/plugins/ingested_llama_3p1_8b.hpp:31-33, :48; gen/src/tron/h/tron/plugins/qwen_3_4b_instruct_2507.hpp:31-33, :48]: inline 16,384 + 131,072 + 131,072 + 8,192 = 286,720 bytes (280 KiB); DMA memory 1024 x 8,192 = 8,388,608 bytes (8 MiB); footprint 8,675,328 bytes (8.27 MiB). On the float host executor btensor_t is tensor<N> with an inline float array, so there v_stars is 128 x 4096 x 4 = 2,097,152 bytes inline; that executor is never AMX-eligible [common.hpp:96; h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:31-34; amx_attn_iface.hpp:156-164]. t_llama_unit builds its model on that float executor and heap-copies v_stars (not attn_accum) because the inline arrays overflow the stack; the sentence says nothing about the bf16 executors [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:1406-1407, :1541-1545].

Instance count. attn has app_pool().num_workers() elements, a runtime value equal to the app CPU count minus one [self_attention.hpp:456, :472; threading.hpp:868-869]; only the first n_attn_workers are attention scratchpads (:993 passes worker_ix; :798 maps it to the last pool workers), so num_workers - n_attn_workers elements are never used by attention. Each hw_attn_results holds two more attn_accum (data and unpack_tmp) plus a max_minibatch_size array of atomic ints; hw_attn holds one hw_attn_results per hardware-attention pass, constructed by emplace_back, and stays empty when no pass is built; these copies are accessed through hw_attn_results_at, never by apply_page_range [self_attention.hpp:391-399, :440-447, :462; model.hpp:2745-2748]. attn and hw_attn belong to self_attention::state, one per model::state; target and draft in speculative decoding each have one [model.hpp:1149-1150; self_attention.hpp:258-259, :270-273]. The "~1MB" remark in the hw_attn comment [self_attention.hpp:458-461] counts something else (the lazily allocated raw DMA landing buffers) and is not contradicted here.

Cost of the array form (Ben's literal proposal), computed from constants. One slot is 4096 bytes [amx_attn_iface.hpp:141, :118; self_attention.hpp:1324-1326]. A std::array<amx_qpack_slot, max_minibatch_size> member is inline: 128 x 4096 = 524,288 bytes (512 KiB) for the llama plugin, 1024 x 4096 = 4,194,304 bytes (4 MiB) for the mock plugin and every generated plugin [llama.hpp:184; h/tron/plugins/mock.hpp:98; TronCpp.hs:264]. Per attn_accum for the llama-3.1-8b shape: inline size 35,840 -> 560,128 bytes (15.6x); footprint 1,084,416 -> 1,608,704 bytes (+48.3%). For the 1024-token generated plugins: inline 286,720 -> 4,481,024 bytes (15.6x); footprint 8,675,328 -> 12,869,632 bytes (+48.3%). On the float host executor the inline growth is +25% of a 2 MiB struct. The cost is paid in every attn_accum: all num_workers elements of attn (including the never-used ones), both copies in every hw_attn_results (which never run apply_page_range), and every model type compiled with the option whether or not it can take the AMX path; ineligible shapes include head size other than 128 (llama_3p2_1b, gpt-oss: 64), kv_mul other than 4 (llama_3p1_70b: 8; llama_2_13b: 1) and the host and host_fp16 executors [amx_attn_iface.hpp:147-164; llama.hpp:1560, :1565-1566; common.hpp:96-98]. Value-initialization zero-fills the array at attn.resize and hw_attn.emplace_back; that construction cost is not measured. Total for a process = (number of model states) x (num_workers + 2 x hw_attn.size()) x max_minibatch_size x 4096 bytes. For the pool we run on delphi-3bda (Xeon 6962P, --instance 1,2: 56 CPU ids minus one = 55 pool workers, threading.hpp:869) that is 55 x 512 KiB = 28,835,840 bytes (27.5 MiB) per model state for the llama plugin and 55 x 4 MiB = 230,686,720 bytes (220 MiB) for the ingested plugins, plus 1 MiB or 8 MiB per hw_attn_results (two copies) when a hardware pass exists; computed from the declared types and that machine's 55-worker pool, not measured as resident memory (numbers agree with the parallel page, section 7).

Cost of the vector form. A std::vector<amx_qpack_slot> member adds 24 bytes inline per attn_accum (three pointers, libstdc++ x86-64) and allocates only in scratchpads that run the AMX path, to items.size() x 4096 bytes, keeping its capacity (the allocation pattern of the current thread_local, now owned by the struct). It keeps the runtime allocation Ben flagged; his amortization argument (long-lived scratchpad, bounded batch) applies unchanged. Where the thread_local is one vector per OS thread per instantiation shared by every state of that model type, the member is one per attn_accum per state; with one model state per process the counts of allocated vectors are equal, with several states (speculative decoding) the member form holds one per state, allocated only where AMX ran.

The two prototypes (worktree ~/workspace/ai-runs/tron-qpack-accum, read-only, local and unpushed, on top of 26a0338c3b). 5b5eeca67f (vector) moves amx_qpack_slot to state scope [self_attention.hpp:277-285 @ 5b5eeca67f], adds std::vector<amx_qpack_slot> amx_qpack as an attn_accum member under #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [:299-309 @ 5b5eeca67f], replaces the thread_local with auto& amx_qpack = attn[scratchpad].amx_qpack [:1337 @ 5b5eeca67f], keeps the per-call resize(items.size()) [:1348 @ 5b5eeca67f] and rewords two comments; self_attention.hpp only, 28 insertions and 18 deletions (git diff --stat 26a0338c3b 5b5eeca67f). Its new comment says "Only this worker touches attn[scratchpad] during this call" [:1335-1336 @ 5b5eeca67f], which carries the same over-broad ownership claim as the draft and must be corrected before any push. 250ff8204d (array, on top of the vector) replaces the member with std::array<amx_qpack_slot, max_minibatch_size> amx_qpack [:306 @ 250ff8204d] and deletes the resize block from the if (amx_on) branch [:1341-1344 @ 250ff8204d]; 4 insertions, 10 deletions; same comment at :1333-1334 @ 250ff8204d. Both subjects start with "PROTOTYPE (verification only)"; only the vector commit's body says "Not for merging as is", the array commit's body says "Built and tested for comparison only"; both bodies name the reviewer, so a real commit message must be written before any push (git -C ~/workspace/ai-runs/tron-qpack-accum log -2 --format=%B). The vector prototype's member comment (:299-308 @ 5b5eeca67f) already says "never joined" and matches the page's replacement comment in substance; the array prototype's member lacks the {} initializer the page recommends for option A (:306 @ 250ff8204d). Both built on delphi-3bda (Xeon 6962P) with TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON, rc 0 in 120 s (array) and 126 s (vector), and passed t_amx_numerics (4109 assertions in 4 cases), t_amx_dispatch_dtype (1559 in 1) and t_llama_unit (120054 in 27); the vector form also built with the option OFF (rc 0, 131 s) and passed t_llama_unit [~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/logs/split-qpackA.txt, split-qpackB.txt, split-qpackBoff.txt]. No performance measurement exists for either form, nor for the current placement against them.

Packer contract both placements satisfy: a 4096-byte buffer aligned to 64 bytes with columns 4..15 of every panel zero; the packer writes columns 0..3 only, so a buffer zeroed once stays valid [amx_attn_iface.hpp:171-179]. resize zero-fills new vector slots; value-initialization zero-fills the array.

thread_local subtlety. apply_page_range is a member function template of self_attention<model>::state with two explicit parameters (geometry, query_buffer_t) and a third deduced one, the page-range container type of the const auto& page_range parameter [self_attention.hpp:217, :258-259, :1263-1270]. A function-local thread_local is one object per instantiation per thread, constructed the first time that thread passes the declaration. Production uses one container type, std::vector<page_id> (kv_section::page_ixs), so the count is one per (geometry, query buffer type); the tests add std::span instantiations [common.hpp:737; self_attention.hpp:993-995; t_llama_unit.cpp:1537-1539; t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:149-150]. The handwritten llama path instantiates once per model type (legacy_geometry, qs_for_attn) [self_attention.hpp:878-880]; generated plugins instantiate once per attention geometry [model.hpp:2173-2186; TronCpp.hs:1282-1285, :1322-1326]. In the gen/ snapshot (2026-08-27/30) 13 of 15 generated headers use one attention geometry; the two gemma-4 headers use two geometries each (head sizes 512 and 256, neither eligible), so two instantiations per model type there. The declaration sits outside the if constexpr (amx_eligible) branch, so ineligible instantiations also give each thread that runs them a 24-byte empty vector (never resized, since amx_on is false) [self_attention.hpp:1311, :1327, :1377-1381]. The parallel page's claim C6 (read off the 2026-08-31 runtron binary, which declares the buffer the same way; not re-verified here) counts 74 instantiations, a 24-byte vector plus an 8-byte init guard of static thread-local storage per (thread x instantiation), 7 thread-local-storage instructions per apply_page_range call and none inside the page and token loops, and one __cxa_thread_atexit destructor registration per thread per instantiation, which also happens when the AMX path is off.

Conclusion. Ben is right: the buffer can live in attn_accum. Nothing in the ownership scheme prevents the slots from living there; the current comment's reason ("per (token, kv_head)"; "ownership unchanged") is a scope statement about this PR's diff, not a technical obstacle. Recommendation: option B, the std::vector member sized on first use (prototype 5b5eeca67f); option A, the std::array member (prototype 250ff8204d), also builds and passes. The detailed analysis with nine verified claims, the cost tables and a suggested code comment is the parallel page ~/workspace/intel-AMX/PR3879/new-PRs/PR1/reviews/amx_qpack-2-attn_accum.html (section 8 has the suggested reply and the replacement comment). Moving the buffer into attn_accum makes its owner visible in a struct instead of in thread-local storage, which is Ben's point. The vector member keeps the allocation pattern and costs 24 bytes per attn_accum plus heap only where AMX runs; the array member removes the allocation but adds 512 KiB or 4 MiB inline to every attn_accum of every model built with the option. A fourth form, an array member only when the plugin's shape and executor are eligible (a struct-level constant from plugin_t::attention_kernels and model::activation_scalar, pattern at model.hpp:723-728 and :827), would remove the cost for ineligible models but still put 4 MiB in every scratchpad of an eligible generated plugin, including the hw_attn_results copies and the unused attn elements.

See also: Detailed investigation with the two prototype builds: amx_qpack-2-attn_accum.html

Proposed code change

Option B (recommended): cherry-pick 5b5eeca67f (worktree ~/workspace/ai-runs/tron-qpack-accum, read-only, on top of 26a0338c3b) onto jhan-amx-p0; h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp only, 28 insertions and 18 deletions: move struct alignas(64) amx_qpack_slot from apply_page_range (:1324-1326 at head) to state scope under #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (:277-285 in the prototype); add std::vector<amx_qpack_slot> amx_qpack; as an attn_accum member under the same #ifdef (:299-309 in the prototype); replace thread_local std::vector<amx_qpack_slot> amx_qpack; (:1327) with auto& amx_qpack = attn[scratchpad].amx_qpack;; keep the per-call amx_qpack.resize(items.size()) (:1338 at head). Two edits before the push: (1) the local comment 'Only this worker touches attn[scratchpad] during this call' (:1333-1336 @ 5b5eeca67f) is over-broad, because joiners write attn[0]'s v_stars, s_stars, m_stars and valid while worker 0 may still be in a sections call (:1060-1063, :1094-1095); write 'Only this worker touches attn[scratchpad].amx_qpack; the join phase writes other members of attn[0]' instead, or use the page's replacement comment (section 8 of ~/workspace/intel-AMX/PR3879/new-PRs/PR1/reviews/amx_qpack-2-attn_accum.html) for the member and drop the ownership sentence from the local comment; (2) a real commit message without the reviewer's name (the prototype's says 'PROTOTYPE (verification only)' and names him). Optional, not in the prototype: alignas(64) on the member so the 24-byte vector header never shares a cache line with valid, which joiners write in attn[0] (hypothesis only, see insufficient_data). Option A (the reviewer's literal ask): cherry-pick 250ff8204d on top of B; std::array<amx_qpack_slot, max_minibatch_size> member and the resize block removed (:306, :1341-1344 @ 250ff8204d); 4 insertions and 10 deletions; write the member as amx_qpack{} so zero-initialization does not depend on value-initialization of the enclosing struct (page claim C7); same comment and commit-message edits; adds 512 KiB (llama plugin) or 4 MiB (ingested plugins) inline to every attn_accum.

Insufficient data

  • Decode and prefill throughput of the vector-member and array-member forms versus 26a0338c3b: needs the qwen-3-4b tp2 8 users prompt 8192 regression run (protocol of 2026-09-09, exec/results/qwen8u8k-pr1-half-*.txt) on each build; that round's clean-versus-kill-switch spread of +0.6% (14.650 versus 14.745 tokens/s per user) bounds what one run can resolve, so a difference below about 1% needs repeats.
  • Per-call cost of the current thread_local access versus indexing attn[scratchpad]: hypothesis (a function-local thread_local with a non-trivial constructor carries a per-access initialization check), not cited to code; needs a microbenchmark of apply_page_range or perf counters on the attention workers.
  • Construction-time cost of the array form: attn.resize (self_attention.hpp:472) and hw_attn.emplace_back (model.hpp:2748) would zero-fill an extra 512 KiB (llama plugin) or 4 MiB (generated plugins) per attn_accum next to the 128 or 1024 DMA allocations that already happen; the hw_attn comment's '~0.8 ms per element' (:458-461) describes the current layout; no measurement of either exists.
  • Total bytes the array form would add in a process: needs the number of model states, app_pool().num_workers() (= app CPU count minus one, threading.hpp:868-869) and hw_attn.size() of that configuration; formula (states) x (num_workers + 2 x hw_attn.size()) x max_minibatch_size x 4096 bytes.
  • Whether the shipped rinzler binaries instantiate apply_page_range more than once per model: the gen/ header snapshot (2026-08-27/30) shows one attention geometry in 13 of 15 generated headers and two in the gemma-4 headers; a symbol count (nm) on a built rinzler would confirm for the shipped set.
  • False sharing between joiners' writes to attn[0].valid and the vector header of attn[0].amx_qpack in the vector form: hypothesis; depends on the layout (for scratch_kv_heads 8 the header starts on its own 64-byte line, computed from member sizes) and would need a perf-counter measurement or an alignas(64) on the member to rule out.
  • Exact sizeof(attn_accum) on the tron toolchain: the 35,840-byte (llama plugin) and 286,720-byte (generated plugins) inline figures are computed from the declared types (handle 16 bytes, vector header 24 bytes, from a standalone replica on g++ 11.4, scratchpad/handle_sizes.cpp; the parallel page's claim C3 reproduces the totals with its own standalone compile); a sizeof print or static_assert in a tron build would give the exact value.
  • Resident memory of the two prototype binaries under runtron: the 27.5 MiB and 220 MiB per-model-state figures for the array form are computed from the declared types and the 55-worker pool, not measured; a resident-set comparison of the three builds (head, 5b5eeca67f, 250ff8204d) would confirm them.

Reply to post

You were right, and the comment above the declaration (self_attention.hpp:1319-1321 at 26a0338c3b) gave a scope reason, "this PR keeps attn_accum's ownership unchanged", in the form of a technical one; sorry for the confusion. A pack (the 4096-byte copy of one token's four query heads in the layout the AMX tile instructions read, :1324-1326) is per-worker, per-token scratch of one `apply_page_range` call, which is exactly the index space of `attn_accum` (the per-worker scratchpad struct that holds every token's running attention state, :280-288). Nothing outside that call reads a pack: the join phase writes only `v_stars`, `s_stars`, `m_stars` and `valid` of the target scratchpad (:1094-1095, :1129-1132, :1700-1750), and no file other than self_attention.hpp names the buffer. The buffer is now a `std::vector<amx_qpack_slot>` member of `attn_accum`, sized on first use with `resize(items.size())`, and the thread_local is gone (commit HASH, fill in after the push). The allocation pattern is unchanged: a worker allocates only when a minibatch (the tokens one forward pass serves together) is larger than every earlier one it has seen, so the amortization you described still applies. With the AMX build option on, t_amx_numerics, t_amx_dispatch_dtype and t_llama_unit pass on the Xeon 6962P with the same assertion counts as the unmodified head, and t_llama_unit also passes with the option off. I kept a vector rather than `std::array<amx_qpack_slot, max_minibatch_size>` because of what the array costs, computed from the declared types and that machine's 55-worker pool (56 CPU ids minus one under `--instance 1,2`, threading.hpp:869). The array adds 512 KiB (llama plugin, `max_minibatch_size` 128, llama.hpp:184) or 4 MiB (ingested plugins, 1024, ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:264) to every `attn_accum` instance, whose inline size today is 35,840 bytes (llama-3.1-8b) or 286,720 bytes (Qwen3-4B). That cost is paid in all 55 pool-worker scratchpads of a model state (:456, :472), 27.5 MiB or 220 MiB per model state, in both copies inside every `hw_attn_results` (the scratchpad of one hardware-attention pass, :392, :399), and in every model built with the option whether or not it can take the AMX path. The vector member costs 24 bytes in every scratchpad where the AMX path never runs. The array form also builds and passes the same three tests (a second local prototype); if you prefer it over the vector member, say so and it goes in instead.

3. Ben: Helper extraction; range_hint in the AMX path

Replied and resolved   Action: Nothing left to do  |  open on GitHub

Replied by jhan 2026-09-10 22:37 UTC (comment 3984107071) and resolved; the two-line comment landed as 524c510609. The text below is the draft the reply was based on; its line numbers are for 26a0338c3b.

The helper Ben asked for exists at head (three functions; apply_page_range 172 -> 145 lines, apply_page_tok 224 -> 139 lines, against 91 / 119 at the merge base a80b102c18, signature to closing brace). His follow-up on range_hint is answered from the code (a dense page lies inside the hinted range in full, so the dotter would never advance the hint either; the returned pointer equals the dotter's) and from measured data: pages with a range boundary inside them were 60.0 / 9.09 / 2.33 % of prefill page visits at prompt 256 / 2048 / 8192 tokens and 0 % of decode page visits (2026-09-01, qwen-3-4b, one user, USE_HW_ATTN=0, mirror arm of the measurement branch 3cd2670df1; inferred for this PR because the dense gate has the same five conditions in both arms). Two optional additions are offered, none applied: a two-line comment at the AMX return (:1597) and a host test (22 added / 4 removed lines, drafted in the scratchpad, not run). No production code change is required. Aligned with the parallel page ~/workspace/intel-AMX/PR3879/new-PRs/PR1/reviews/range_hint.html (section 6 = suggested reply + comment).

The comment

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3936963481, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1451 (old head 60d66d9c04)

Can we not move this out into a distinct helper? This function is already coming up on 200 LoC; let's not make a bad situation even worse.

Ben, 2026-09-09, comment 3973407196, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1451 (old head 60d66d9c04)

Thank you for the refactoring! This looks better; let's just make sure that we shouldn't be handling range_hint in the AMX path as well.

What was asked

Ben's first comment (2026-09-04, on the old head 60d66d9c04) asked to move the AMX block (the query pack inside apply_page_range, the function that runs one attention worker over a range of KV pages) into a separate helper, because apply_page_range was approaching 200 lines. His second comment (2026-09-09, written after the current head 26a0338c3b was pushed) thanks for the refactoring and asks to make sure the AMX path does not also need to handle range_hint (the pointer to the current token_range, i.e. the interval of KV token ids that share one visibility mask, which the AVX loop advances as it walks the tokens of a page).

What is true at the head (4290402491)

Part A, the helper. Ben's anchor line 1451 at the old head sits inside apply_page_range, which then ran from line 1314 to 1485, 172 lines, of which 60 lines were inside three TRON_AMX_DISPATCH blocks [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1314-1485 @ 60d66d9c04]. At the head, apply_page_range runs from 1264 to 1408, 145 lines, 53 of them inside three TRON_AMX_DISPATCH blocks [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1264, :1408, :1300-1341, :1376-1383, :1405-1407]. The first block (buffer type, thread_local vector, bitset, resize, begin_region) is 42 lines: 14 code, 26 comment, 2 preprocessor [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1300-1341]. The second block is the pack call, 8 lines [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1376-1383]. The third block is the end_region call, 3 lines [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1405-1407]. apply_page_tok runs from 1542 to 1680, 139 lines, 20 of them in one TRON_AMX_DISPATCH block (the gate), against 224 lines (1493-1716, 99 inside TRON_AMX_DISPATCH) at the old head [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1542, :1680, :1581-1600; :1493-1716 @ 60d66d9c04].

The base of comparison must be named, because main moved after the PR branched. At the PR's merge base a80b102c18 (which equals the worktree's local main 9b11a912ba; git diff between the two is empty for this file) apply_page_range is 91 lines (1253-1343) and apply_page_tok is 119 lines (1351-1469) [a80b102c18:h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1253-1343, :1351-1469]. On origin/main 815ce9253c (fetched 2026-09-10 00:45 UTC, after PR #4267 merged) the file has 16 more lines: the page_share_counters.hpp include and five TRON_PAGE_SHARE_COUNTERS blocks, all five inside apply_page_range; apply_page_range is 106 lines (1254-1359) there and apply_page_tok is unchanged at 119 lines (1367-1485) [git diff a80b102c18 815ce9253c -- h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp; 815ce9253c:h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1254-1359, :1367-1485]. The read-only worktree's origin/main ref resolved to 47760c7533 (the merge of #4265) at 2026-09-10 ~18:30 UTC, not to 815ce9253c as facts.md recorded; the two functions have the same lengths there (106 / 119) [47760c7533:h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1254-1359, :1367-1485]. A rebase onto 815ce9253c or later adds those 15 lines to this branch's apply_page_range (145 + 15 = 160 lines, computed, not measured on a rebased tree). Method for every length: lines counted from the signature line to the closing brace at class-member indentation (the line that is exactly four spaces and a brace), inclusive; blank, comment and preprocessor lines included (re-verified 2026-09-10 by printing the signature and brace lines at 26a0338c3b, 60d66d9c04, a80b102c18, 815ce9253c, 47760c7533).

The AMX code now lives in three functions: packed_amx_query, 14 lines, packs one token's query group once per call [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1417-1430]; is_dense_amx_page, 12 lines, the predicate [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1440-1451]; apply_dense_amx_page, 70 lines, QK, the scale/max/exp step, PV and the running-softmax update [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1464-1533]. The gate inside apply_page_tok is 10 code lines plus an 8-line comment and 2 preprocessor lines: the packed-Q null test and the predicate, then the call and the return [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1581-1600, :1590-1599]. Commit 69031c201d moved the predicate from the helper (then try_apply_dense_amx_page, 14 parameters) to the caller and renamed it, stating no behavior change [git show 69031c201d: 29 insertions, 30 deletions in h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp]. The open attn_accum thread (3936949728 / 3973410716) decides part of the remaining length: in the read-only prototype, apply_page_range measures 134 lines (1285-1418, 42 inside TRON_AMX_DISPATCH) with amx_qpack as a std::vector member of attn_accum (commit 5b5eeca67f) and 130 lines (1283-1412, 38 inside) with a std::array member (commit 250ff8204d), same method [~/workspace/ai-runs/tron-qpack-accum, 5b5eeca67f and 250ff8204d:h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp]. This prototype sentence is kept out of the reply (budget; it belongs to the attn_accum item).

Part B, range_hint. The parallel page ~/workspace/intel-AMX/PR3879/new-PRs/PR1/reviews/range_hint.html (same author, 2026-09-10) covers this question in depth: its conclusion is 'range_hint is a bookmark, not a decision; the AMX path returns it unchanged, which equals the dotter's value for a dense page'; its section 4.2 lists six claims C1-C6 that 3 verifier lenses each did not refute; its section 4.3 has the measured fallback shares; its section 6 has the suggested reply and a two-line comment for the AMX return at :1597. This item agrees with that page.

A token_range is a half-open interval [tok_lo, tok_hi) of KV token ids with one pair of masks (SW_REQUIRED, HW_AVAILABLE) for every token in it [h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:97-106]. visible(job_ix) and sw_required(job_ix) test the query's bit in those masks, so the query's visibility is one value per range [h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:158-160, :163-165]. add_range asserts that ranges are sorted and non-overlapping; find_range with a hint asserts that adjacent ranges have no gap [h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:211-214, :254-256]. find_range(tok, hint) returns the range containing tok: the hint itself if tok is inside it, else a forward walk of at most 4 ranges, else a binary search that asserts containment [h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:240-264, :220-235]. So the hint is a search accelerator: any valid hint gives the correct range, a poor hint costs at most one binary search.

apply_page_tok has exactly one caller, apply_page_range [git grep apply_page_tok at 26a0338c3b: the only call is h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1384]. That caller computes page_begin_hint = find_range(page.at_offset(0), *page_end_hint) once per page, so the pointer handed to apply_page_tok is the range containing the page's first token [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1347-1348]. apply_page_tok computes query_visible from that range once, before the gate [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1579-1580]. The dotter loop advances range_hint when kv_idx = page.at_offset(i) reaches range_hint->tok_hi and recomputes query_visible [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1621-1627]; it returns the pair (relevant_k_tokens, final pointer) [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1637, :1679], and the caller stores the pointer as page_end_hint, the search hint for the next page [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1387]. The AMX path returns the pair (64, incoming pointer unchanged) and contains no find_range call [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1592-1597].

Why unchanged is correct: is_dense_amx_page requires begin == 0, end == page_size, query_visible, pg.at_offset(page_size - 1) < range.tok_hi, and q_position - k_position < sliding_window_size [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1448-1450]. at_offset(i) returns token_id(lo + i), so the 64 token ids of a page are consecutive and increasing [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1231-1233, :1494-1497]. The first token is inside the range (find_range contract) and the last is below tok_hi, so all 64 are inside [tok_lo, tok_hi) of one range; the dotter's advance condition kv_idx >= tok_hi is false for every i, so the dotter would also return the incoming pointer [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1623, :1449]. The first element of the pair matches too: for a page with begin == 0, end == 64, query visible throughout and every offset inside the window, the dotter increments relevant_k_tokens once per offset and returns 64 [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1620, :1637, :1679], the value the AMX path returns as int(page::page_size) [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1597]. The same tok_hi check existed at the old head, so the refactor did not change range handling [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1569-1571 @ 60d66d9c04].

Sliding window: the dotter skips token i when (int)q_position - (int)(k_position + i) >= sliding_window_size, where k_position is the position of the token at offset 0, so the dotter itself treats positions inside a page as k_position + i [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1564, :1633; a80b102c18:h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1422]. The predicate tests offset 0 (q_position - k_position < sliding_window_size); q_position - (k_position + i) is smaller for every i > 0, so all later offsets pass too [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1450]. With no window, sliding_window_size equals illegal_sliding_window = INT_MAX: illegal_sliding_window is defined at self_attention.hpp:207, attn_layer_config defaults to it [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:212], bind_attention_runtime_config assigns operation.config.sliding_window_size.value_or(illegal_sliding_window) or copies the layer's value [h/tron/models/model.hpp:648-651, :659-660, :671-672], the no-window test elsewhere is equality with illegal_sliding_window [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:500], and apply_page_range reads the bound value [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1292-1294]. The EAGLE case (begin = 1) fails begin == 0 and takes the dotter [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1572, :1448].

Straddling pages, measured: a full 64-token page whose last token id is >= tok_hi fails the predicate and runs the dotter loop, which handles the boundary as at the PR base [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1449, :1620-1632]. How often that happens WAS measured, once, with per-cause fallback counters (build flag -DTRON_AMX_FALLBACK_COUNTERS=1) on the measurement branch jhan-amx-fence at commit 3cd2670df1, mirror arm (-DTRON_AMX_K_MIRROR=ON), qwen-3-4b tp2, one user, 256 generated tokens, USE_HW_ATTN=0, two repetitions per prompt length with identical counts, 2026-09-01 [~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/results/fallback-20260901/fallback.txt:6-7, :16-17, :26-27; run-c256-r1.log:24 (Version 2026.09.01-3cd2670d-jhan-amx-fence), :100 (USE_HW_ATTN=0); ~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/fence-20260831/run-fallback.sh:26, :43]. Result, share of page visits whose first failing gate condition was 'range boundary inside the page' (range_cut): prefill 60.00 % (110,592 of 184,320) at prompt 256, 9.09 % (884,736 of 9,732,096) at prompt 2048, 2.33 % (3,538,944 of 152,174,592) at prompt 8192; decode 0 % at all three prompt lengths (decode's only fallback is the partial tail page: 15.22 / 2.86 / 0.76 %). No visit failed for a missing Q pack, an invisible query, a window edge or a missing mirror plane, so range_cut there equals 'a boundary inside a full, visible page'. Caveat: the run was on the mirror arm; the fence gate tests the same five conditions as is_dense_amx_page (inline, plus one mirror-plane condition that never failed), and PR1's diff does not touch the range structure or the batching, so the shares carry over to PR1 by reasoning, not by re-measurement (see insufficient_data). The parallel page reconciles the exact counts with the construction rule under the assumption of 128 query tokens per prefill pass (36 layers x 8 KV heads); the source of that pass size was not traced.

Where boundaries come from: in the builder, each query event creates a range break (causality) and each offloadable shard creates an SW-to-HW transition [doc/hw-kv-ranged-mask.md:146-148]; each entry of an edge first gets its own range, encoding causality and subtree membership [doc/hw-kv-ranged-mask.md:172-176]; finalize merges adjacent ranges with identical masks [doc/hw-kv-ranged-mask.md:154-155]. So a boundary survives where the mask changes: at a query position, at a branch point of the KV tree, or at an SW-to-HW transition. For one decoding sequence whose query is its last token, all its earlier KV tokens carry the same mask and form one range (the doc's example merges its 20 prompt entries into one range [doc/hw-kv-ranged-mask.md:228-229]); the measured decode range_cut = 0 for one user is consistent with this. Multi-user decode, shared prefixes (a parent's tail and its first child's head can share a page) and FPGA-attention-on runs were not measured. The page-share counters of #4267 are not on this branch: the head has no page_share_counters.hpp and no PAGE_SHARE reference in self_attention.hpp [git ls-tree 26a0338c3b h/tron/kernels/page_share_counters.hpp: empty; git grep -i page_share 26a0338c3b -- h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp: no match].

Tests: t_amx_dispatch_dtype builds one range [0, n_tokens) per case with add_range, which does not merge [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:115-120; h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:207-216]; its cases are a full page (one pack, one QK, one PV), a 63-token page (one pack, no kernel), and float / fp16 executors (no pack) [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:136-138, :193-209]. Its probe counts pack, QK and PV calls and checks the packed values; it cannot observe the pointer apply_page_tok returns [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:152-160, :174-188]. No case puts a range boundary inside a full page. A draft section (22 added lines, 4 removed, grep-counted) is in the scratchpad file proposed-test-range-break.diff, not applied, not run; its full text is in proposed_code_change. t_llama_unit's "apply and join page ranges" builds a per-query range structure but uses the float executor (host = make_llm_executor<false, tensor, tensor, tensor>) with kv_mul 2, so the AMX gate is never reached there [t/t_llama_unit.cpp:1375, :1381, :1385-1386, :1407, :1485-1499; h/tron/models/common.hpp:96].

See also: Full audit of range_hint with the measured boundary shares: range_hint.html

Proposed code change

No production code change is required. Two optional additions, both drafted, not applied, not run.

(1) Two-line comment at the AMX return, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1597 at 26a0338c3b (from the parallel page range_hint.html, section 6):
        // The hint is returned unchanged: is_dense_amx_page proved that the page's last
        // token is below range_hint->tok_hi, so the dotter's advance (kv_idx >= tok_hi
        // below) could not have fired for this page either.
        return {int(page::page_size), range_hint};

(2) Host test in t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp (drafted, not applied, not run; 22 added / 4 removed lines by grep; needs a DISPATCH=ON build and a run of t_amx_dispatch_dtype on delphi-3bda). It verifies that a full page with a range boundary inside it takes the dotter loop (one pack, no QK/PV call); it cannot verify the returned pointer, because the probe counts pack/QK/PV calls only (t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:152-160). The two ranges differ only in another query's bit, the production shape of a fully visible straddling page; add_range does not merge them (ranged_mask.hpp:207-216), whereas production finalize would merge identical masks (doc/hw-kv-ranged-mask.md:154-155). Insufficient data

  • The range-boundary shares on this PR's own build (canonical arm, 26a0338c3b) are not re-measured: the per-cause counters live on the measurement branch jhan-amx-fence (3cd2670df1) and were run on its mirror arm only (2026-09-01). The shares carry over by reasoning (same five gate conditions; PR1's diff does not touch the range structure or batching). Measurement that would close it: rebuild the fence instrumentation (or a range_cut counter next to the #4267 page-share counters, h/tron/kernels/page_share_counters.hpp on origin/main 815ce9253c, near total_page_visits at :40) on top of 26a0338c3b, DISPATCH=ON, and rerun qwen-3-4b tp2, one user, prompt 256 / 2048 / 8192 on delphi-3bda; the counters are not on this branch (no page_share_counters.hpp at 26a0338c3b), so #4267 must be merged into the branch or the branch rebased first.
  • The shares with FPGA attention on (USE_HW_ATTN unset), where hardware-offload events add range boundaries, and in decode with several users or shared prefixes (a parent's tail and its first child's head can share one page), per cause. The 2026-09-01 run had one user and USE_HW_ATTN=0. Measurement: the same counter build on an FPGA host with USE_HW_ATTN unset, and a multi-user run (8 users, prompt 8192) with the counters split by phase.
  • The wall-clock cost of straddling pages compared with the AMX path. They run the pre-PR dotter loop, so their per-page cost equals the pre-PR cost by construction; the missed gain would be measured with the same counter build by timing apply_page_tok for straddling pages against dense pages on delphi-3bda. No such number exists in the inputs for this item.
  • Where the 128-query-tokens-per-prefill-pass split comes from (the parallel page reproduces the measured counts exactly under that assumption; the qwen plugin's own limit is 1024 query tokens per minibatch). It affects only the reconciliation of the counts, not the safety argument. Measurement: a log of the query count per prefill pass in the scheduler.

Reply to post

The AMX (Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions, the CPU tile-multiply instructions) code is now three functions in h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp at 26a0338c3b: `packed_amx_query` (:1417-1430, 14 lines) packs one token's query group (its 4 query heads) once, `is_dense_amx_page` (:1440-1451, 12 lines) decides whether a (query, page) pair may take the AMX path, and `apply_dense_amx_page` (:1464-1533, 70 lines) runs the page. Counted from the signature line to the closing brace with comments included, `apply_page_range` (one attention worker's loop over a range of KV pages, the 64-token blocks of the cached key and value vectors) is 145 lines (:1264-1408), against 172 at 60d66d9c04 and 91 at the merge base a80b102c18. By the same count `apply_page_tok` (one query token against one page) is 139 lines (:1542-1680), against 224 and 119. What remains of AMX in `apply_page_range` is the packed-query storage plus the tile region (:1300-1341 and :1405-1407, the span between `begin_region` and `end_region`, which load and release the tile configuration) and the pack call (:1376-1383); in `apply_page_tok` it is the gate (:1590-1599).

On `range_hint`: it is a bookmark, not a decision, because it points at the entry of the ranged mask (the per-pass table of KV token ranges [tok_lo, tok_hi) that share one visibility mask, h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:97-106) where the last lookup landed, so that the next lookup costs one comparison instead of a binary search. `find_range` with a hint checks the token against the range it returns (ranged_mask.hpp:240-264), so a stale hint costs time and never changes a result. The AMX path is entered only when the whole page lies inside the hinted range: the caller passes the range containing the page's first token (:1347-1348), `is_dense_amx_page` requires the page's last token `at_offset(63)` to be below that range's `tok_hi` (:1449), and token ids inside a page are consecutive (`at_offset(i) == lo + i`, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1231-1233). For such a page the dotter loop (the existing per-token loop written with AVX-512, the 512-bit vector instructions) would never take its advance branch, which fires only when a token id reaches `tok_hi` (:1621-1627). So the hint the AMX path returns unchanged (:1597) equals the hint the dotter returns (:1679), and the caller's `page_end_hint` sequence (:1387) is identical with AMX on or off. The AMX path therefore needs no `range_hint` handling, and it adds no `find_range` call (:1592-1597), so the hint mechanism runs exactly as before. A full page with a range boundary inside it fails the predicate and runs the dotter loop as before this PR. How often that happens was measured once with per-cause counters on a measurement branch (commit 3cd2670df1, qwen-3-4b, one user, USE_HW_ATTN=0, 2026-09-01): 60.0 / 9.09 / 2.33 % of prefill page visits (prefill = processing the prompt) at prompt 256 / 2048 / 8192 tokens had a boundary inside the page, and 0 % of decode page visits (decode = generating one token per step) did. Those counters ran on the K-mirror arm of that branch (a build with a second copy of K in the AMX layout, not part of this PR); the dense gate tests the same five conditions in both arms, so the shares for this PR are inferred from that run, not re-measured. Two optional additions are drafted, not applied, and the author's decision: a two-line comment at the AMX return (:1597) stating why the unchanged hint is exact, and a host test (a unit test run on the CPU) that puts a boundary inside a full page and expects no kernel call.

4. Ben: Why not mask_t

Done in the code   Action: Post the reply, then resolve  |  open on GitHub

The hand-written uint64_t bitmap Ben commented on is gone; the head uses std::bitset with a comment saying why mask_t cannot be used, and that reason checks out against mask.hpp (no mask_t<1024> exists, and 1024 is the mock and ingested plugins' max_minibatch_size).

The comment

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3936968255, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1374 (old head 60d66d9c04)

Why not use mask_t here?

What was asked

Ben asked why the per-call bitmap (one bit per token of the minibatch, meaning "this token's query group is already packed for the AMX kernel") was a hand-written uint64_t array instead of mask_t, tron's own fixed-width bit-set type in h/common/mask.hpp. The comment anchored on the old head's line uint64_t amx_packed_mask[(max_minibatch_size + 63) / 64] = {};.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

The hand-written bitmap no longer exists. At the head the flag set is std::bitset<max_minibatch_size> amx_packed, a per-call local whose default constructor clears every bit [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1341]. It was introduced by the dispatch commit b60fd4911e [git log main..HEAD -S'std::bitset<max_minibatch_size>' -- h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp], which also added #include <bitset> [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:5]. The four comment lines above it state why mask_t is not used [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1337-1340]. The declaration sits at function scope inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1320, :1355] and not inside the if constexpr (amx_eligible) guard, which wraps only the call site [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1391-1396]; amx_eligible and amx_on are defined at [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1328-1331]. So the declaration is compiled for every plugin in a build with TRON_AMX_DISPATCH on, AMX-eligible or not. The bitset is passed to packed_amx_query [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1394], whose parameter is std::bitset<max_minibatch_size>& packed [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1429]. That function tests one bit [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1435] and sets it after packing [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1438]. Test and set are the only two operations on the bitset in the file [grep -n 'amx_packed\|packed\.test\|packed\.set' h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp -> :1341 declaration, :1394 argument, :1435 test, :1438 set].

mask_t cannot be used at width 1024, for two independent reasons in the code text. The primary template has static_assert(N >= 8 && N <= 128 && (N & (N - 1)) == 0, "N must be a power of 2 in {8, 16, 32, 64, 128}") [h/common/mask.hpp:252-253]. Its storage type is typename uint__t<N>::type [h/common/mask.hpp:254], and uint__t has specializations for N = 8, 16, 32, 64 and 128 only [h/common/mask.hpp:31-57], so mask_t<1024> has no backing integer type even without the static_assert. struct mask_t is defined exactly three times: the primary template [h/common/mask.hpp:251], the specialization mask_t<512> [h/common/mask.hpp:465] and the specialization written struct alignas(64) mask_t<4096> [h/common/mask.hpp:686]; the two wide ones sit inside #ifdef TRON_AVX512_ENABLED [h/common/mask.hpp:462, :980]. TRON_AVX512_ENABLED is a PUBLIC compile definition set when the CMake option AVX512 is on, by the tron target [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:241-243], the librinzler target [src/rinzler/CMakeLists.txt:53-55] and the pos target [src/pos/CMakeLists.txt:65-67]; the option defaults ON [CMakeLists.txt:44] and a host probe sets it OFF when a required AVX-512 feature is missing [CMakeLists.txt:108-111]. A literal grep for mask_t<1024> over h/, src/, t/ and ingest/ finds zero hits; such a grep cannot exclude a specialization spelled through a named constant, so the three-definitions fact above is the evidence. The only named-constant mask width in use is ranged_mask_capacity = 4096 [h/tron/models/ranged_mask.hpp:20].

max_minibatch_size is set per plugin (the per-model compile-time description tron is built against). self_attention takes it from the model [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:227], which takes it from the plugin [h/tron/models/model.hpp:698]. The llama plugin sets 128 [h/tron/plugins/llama.hpp:184]; the mock plugin sets 1024 [h/tron/plugins/mock.hpp:98]; every ingested plugin (a plugin generated by the ingest tool from a model checkpoint) sets 1024 [ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:264]; the compile-test plugin sets 128 [t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:31]. self_attention.hpp is a template compiled once per plugin, so the declaration at :1341 is compiled with N = 128 and with N = 1024. Both widths have compiled with the bitset in a build with TRON_AMX_DISPATCH on: TRON_AMX_DISPATCH is a PUBLIC compile definition on the tron target [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:190-194]; the CI "Build Tron" step configures with -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON -DBUILD_TEST_MODELS=ON -DBUILD_INGEST_MODELS=ON and builds every target [.github/workflows/cmake-single-platform.yml:365-368], which includes t_generate_mock_plugin (model<mock<host>>) [t/t_generate_mock_plugin.cpp:15; t/CMakeLists.txt:359] and the ingested models; CI was green on the earlier head 26a0338c3b [facts.md]; the four commits after it (752e9f571a, 339dde7fe7, 524c510609, 4290402491) change only self_attention.hpp and leave the bitset declaration and its two uses unchanged [git diff 26a0338c3b 4290402491 -- h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp]. gpt-oss-120b is an ingested plugin (h/tron/plugins holds only llama.hpp and mock.hpp; the run uses runtron model name ingested-gpt-oss-120b-tp2 [~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/gptoss120b-pr1-half.sh:63]) and it ran at the earlier head 26a0338c3b in the DISPATCH=ON build [~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/results/gptoss120b-pr1-half-20260909T1706.txt, header line 1].

The old head's code was uint64_t amx_packed_mask[(max_minibatch_size + 63) / 64] = {}; [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1374 @ 60d66d9c04], read with (amx_packed_mask[batch_job_ix >> 6] >> (batch_job_ix & 63)) & 1 [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1426 @ 60d66d9c04] and written with |= 1ull << (batch_job_ix & 63) [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1446 @ 60d66d9c04]. Under the libstdc++ layout, std::bitset stores its bits in unsigned long words (_GLIBCXX_BITSET_BITS_PER_WORD = __CHAR_BIT__ * __SIZEOF_LONG__, which is 64 bits on x86-64 Linux) [libstdc++ <bitset> header lines 57-60, GCC 11 headers on the workstation], so std::bitset<128> is 2 words = 16 bytes and std::bitset<1024> is 16 words = 128 bytes, computed from the declared types. That equals the old array's uint64_t[2] = 16 bytes and uint64_t[16] = 128 bytes. These are libstdc++ layout facts, not measurements of the tron build: sizeof(std::bitset<N>) is implementation-defined in the C++ standard, and nothing was compiled in the tron build environment here (see insufficient_data).

One small imprecision in the head comment: it says "mask_t<N> exists for N <= 128, 512 and 4096 only" [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1338-1339], while the exact rule is N in {8, 16, 32, 64, 128}, plus 512 and 4096 only when TRON_AVX512_ENABLED is defined [h/common/mask.hpp:252-253, :462]. For the two values in use (128 and 1024) the comment's conclusion holds, so this is an optional wording tweak, not a defect.

Note on the scratchpad checks: bitset_sizes.cpp does not include mask.hpp; it re-evaluates a copy of the constraint expression (accepted for 128, rejected for 1024) and prints sizeof values with the workstation's g++ 11.4; those printed values are superseded by the libstdc++ layout statement above and are no longer cited. mask1024_check.cpp, which would instantiate tron::mask_t<1024>, was never compiled (h/common/assert.hpp pulls in spdlog, which is not installed on this workstation). So the static_assert claim rests on the code text at mask.hpp:252-254 and :31-57, not on a compile.
