Decision for jhan

None required to resolve. Optional: whether to take the one-line comment wording tweak at self_attention.hpp:1338-1339 (the current wording reaches the right conclusion for the values in use, 128 and 1024).

Insufficient data

  • sizeof(std::bitset<128>) and sizeof(std::bitset<1024>) in the tron build itself: the 16-byte and 128-byte figures follow from the libstdc++ layout (unsigned long words, 8 bytes on x86-64), not from a compile in the tron build environment. A one-line static_assert(sizeof(std::bitset<1024>) == 128) compiled inside the tron build (nix develop) would confirm them for the production toolchain.
  • Whether tron::mask_t<1024> actually fails to compile (as opposed to being ruled out by reading mask.hpp:252-254 and :31-57): the scratchpad file mask1024_check.cpp under -DTRY_1024 was never compiled here because h/common/assert.hpp pulls in spdlog, which this workstation lacks. Compiling that file inside the tron build environment (nix develop) would show the static_assert firing.
  • CI status and a gpt-oss-120b run at 4290402491: not checked in this pass (the commit was pushed 2026-09-11 02:32 UTC, after this was written). The compile evidence for both widths (128 and 1024) comes from the earlier head 26a0338c3b; the four later commits (752e9f571a, 339dde7fe7, 524c510609, 4290402491) change only self_attention.hpp and do not touch the bitset declaration or its two uses [git diff 26a0338c3b 4290402491 -- h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp: one file changed, no added or removed line contains amx_packed, packed.test, packed.set or std::bitset]. A local DISPATCH=ON build of 4290402491 on delphi-3bda compiled and passed t_llama_unit (the llama plugin, width 128; 120054 assertions in 27 test cases), t_amx_numerics and t_amx_dispatch_dtype [~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/logs/split-w3edits2-on.txt]; whether that build also compiled a 1024-wide plugin (mock or ingested) is not recorded in that file. A green CI run on 4290402491 would close this.

Reply to post

The hand-written `uint64_t` bitmap has been replaced by `std::bitset<max_minibatch_size> amx_packed` (the C++ standard library's fixed-size bit set), a per-call local (self_attention.hpp:1341). The only operations on it are one test in `packed_amx_query` (self_attention.hpp:1435) and one set after packing (self_attention.hpp:1438).

`mask_t` (tron's fixed-width bit set in h/common/mask.hpp) exists only for N in {8, 16, 32, 64, 128} (mask.hpp:252-254, storage table at :31-57), plus `mask_t<512>` and `mask_t<4096>` when TRON_AVX512_ENABLED is defined (mask.hpp:462, :465, :686). `max_minibatch_size` is set per plugin (the per-model compile-time description tron is built against): 128 in llama.hpp:184, 1024 in mock.hpp:98 and in every ingested plugin (a plugin generated by the ingest tool from a model checkpoint; ingest/src/TronCpp.hs:264). self_attention.hpp is compiled once per plugin, so this declaration is compiled with N = 128 and with N = 1024. The declaration sits inside `#ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH` but outside the `if constexpr (amx_eligible)` guard (self_attention.hpp:1320-1355, guard at :1391), so `mask_t<max_minibatch_size>` would not compile for any 1024-token plugin in a build with the option on. The code needs only test and set on a per-call local, and `std::bitset` provides both. Under the libstdc++ layout (8-byte words on x86-64) `std::bitset<128>` is 16 bytes and `std::bitset<1024>` is 128 bytes, the same storage as the old `uint64_t` array. The comment at self_attention.hpp:1337-1340 records this reason.

5. Ben: Two control-flow paths in apply_page_tok

Done in the code   Action: Post the reply, then resolve  |  open on GitHub

Settled in the AMX walkthrough call of 2026-09-09: Ben opened apply_page_tok and apply_dense_amx_page side by side, first repeated his duplication worry, then saw that the two softmax/commit loops differ, said the split is "not as bad as I thought" and "no, that's fine, I'm okay with this" (transcript 00:50:49-00:52:19), and moved on to range_hint. He asked at 00:56:54 that addressed comments be marked resolved. The thread is still open on GitHub; a two-sentence reply and resolve closes it. No further refactor is requested; the Gemini auto-summary's action item "Refactor duplicated code" is not supported by his words.

The comment

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3936986164, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1505 (old head 60d66d9c04)

The surgery in this function is quite brutal, leading to essentially two distinct control flow paths. This suggests to me that there should be two distinct functions, possibly with commonalities factored out into helpers.

What was asked

Ben (comment 3936986164, on the old head 60d66d9c04 at self_attention.hpp:1505) said the edits to apply_page_tok (the function that applies one KV page to one query token's attention accumulator) were too invasive, because the function now held two separate control-flow paths, the AMX path (Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions tile kernels) and the existing AVX-512 "dotter" loop (the per-token dot-product loop), selected by a flag. He asked for two distinct functions, one per path, with the parts they share moved into helpers.

Post the short reply and resolve. Insufficient data

  • Runtime cost of a 16-parameter apply_dotter_page versus the inline block (option a): not measured; apply_page_tok and apply_dense_amx_page carry TRON(inline) (self_attention.hpp:1558, :1474), but the effect on the kill-switch arm needs the same three-arm run.

Reply to post

As discussed in the walkthrough on 2026-09-09: `apply_page_tok` (h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1559-1701 at 4290402491) now keeps the shared prologue and dispatches dense pages to `apply_dense_amx_page` (:1475-1550); the dotter loop (the existing AVX-512 per-token loop) stays inline and unchanged. You compared the two side by side, noted that the per-head softmax and commit loops differ between the paths (the AMX path folds the PV kernel's output where the dotter folds `scaled_v` inside the same loop), and were OK with the current split. Resolving on that basis; the remaining shared arithmetic (the scale, max, exp and sum step, about 10 identical lines in each path) can become a helper later if you want it.

6. Ben: Syscall constants need a comment

Done in the code   Action: Post the reply, then resolve  |  open on GitHub

The short comment Ben asked for exists at src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:15-26 at head 26a0338c3b, and every fact in it (0x1023, component 18, kernel 5.16, the xstate.rst document) was checked against the Linux kernel headers and documentation at tags v5.15, v5.16 and v6.12; two optional naming nuances remain (the comment's identifier XFEATURE_XTILEDATA is not a kernel spelling, and the document path it names is the one used by kernels v6.12 and later, not by v5.16).

The comment

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3937013971, on src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:17 (old head 60d66d9c04)

These syscall constants deserve a short comment.

What was asked

Ben's comment 3937013971 (2026-09-04) is anchored on the old head's line #define XFEATURE_XTILEDATA 18 (src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:17 @ 60d66d9c04), which followed #ifndef ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM / #define ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM 0x1023 / #endif with no comment at the values. He asked for a short comment saying what these two constants are. They are the two arguments of the arch_prctl system call (a Linux call that sets per-process CPU-architecture state) with which the process asks the kernel for permission to use the AMX (Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions) tile registers; at the old head the interface header described that request in prose (Note [AMX attention dispatch], item 3) but without the values, the kernel version or a document reference.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

Old code, for contrast. At the old head the two constants were bare preprocessor defines with no adjacent comment: #ifndef ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM / #define ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM 0x1023 / #endif / #define XFEATURE_XTILEDATA 18 [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:14-17 @ 60d66d9c04]. Their only use was the permission request syscall(SYS_arch_prctl, ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM, XFEATURE_XTILEDATA) == 0 at the end of the CPU probe [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:67 @ 60d66d9c04]. The old interface header already described the request in prose, as item 3 of the safety chain: "arch_prctl(ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM) - a per-PROCESS grant (Linux xstate rules); result cached" [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:34-37 @ 60d66d9c04]. So what was missing was a comment at the values themselves: what 0x1023 and 18 are, which kernel version has the request, and where it is documented.

Current head. A six-line comment precedes the constants. It says that Linux lets a process use the AMX tile registers only after it asked for them with arch_prctl(ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM, XFEATURE_XTILEDATA), that this exists in kernel 5.16 and later, that it is documented in Documentation/arch/x86/xstate.rst, that both values are part of the kernel ABI, and that the request code is defined locally and guarded rather than taken from <asm/prctl.h>, so the build does not depend on the version of the kernel headers in the toolchain [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:15-20]. The request code stays a #define ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM 0x1023 wrapped in #ifndef [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:21-23]. The component number is now a named constant, constexpr unsigned long xfeature_xtiledata = 18, with its own comment: "XSAVE component number of the AMX tile data (XTILEDATA); the same bit 18 that available() checks in XCR0" [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:24-26]. The comment block and both definitions came in with commit 429283e43d (2026-09-07) [git blame -L 15,26 src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp @ 26a0338c3b]. That commit is the first of the 14 commits of the rebuilt PR above the merge base a80b102c18 [git log --reverse main..HEAD @ 26a0338c3b].

The constants are used once, in detect_and_request(): after the CPUID check (leaf 7.0 EDX bits 22 and 24) and the XCR0 check (bits 17 and 18, the OS-enabled tile state, mask 3u << 17), the probe returns syscall(SYS_arch_prctl, ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM, xfeature_xtiledata) == 0 [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:63-71]. So the comment's "the same bit 18 that available() checks in XCR0" is right about the bit, and loose about the function: the XCR0 test is in detect_and_request() [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:67-70]; available() calls that probe once, through a function-local static, and returns the cached result for the process lifetime [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:76-86, the call at :84]. The head's interface Note item 3 describes the same three checks including "arch_prctl(ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM), the per-process permission Linux requires" [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:42-46].

Kernel verification (saved copies of the kernel files, fetched 2026-09-10, are in the scratchpad as kern-<tag>-<path>). ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM is 0x1023 in the kernel's exported header at tag v6.12, next to ARCH_GET_XCOMP_SUPP 0x1021 and ARCH_GET_XCOMP_PERM 0x1022 [linux arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/prctl.h:13-15 @ v6.12]. The same three defines are at the same lines at tag v5.16, and the v5.15 file (17 lines) has no ARCH_*_XCOMP_* code at all, so "kernel 5.16 and later" is correct for the upstream kernel [linux arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/prctl.h:13-15 @ v5.16; same file lines 1-17 @ v5.15]. The exported header on this machine also defines ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM 0x1023, at line 15; that file is installed by the package linux-libc-dev 5.15.0-187.197 (Ubuntu 22.04.5), not by the running 6.8.0-138-generic kernel, so it is a "5.15" header that already carries the define (a distribution backport) [/usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu/asm/prctl.h:15; dpkg -S and dpkg -l linux-libc-dev; /proc/version]. That header is a concrete case for the #ifndef guard in the source: the toolchain header may or may not define the code, and the guard keeps both cases building.

The XSAVE component number of the tile data is 18. The kernel says so directly: "AMX which requires XFEATURE_XTILE_CFG(17) and XFEATURE_XTILE_DATA(18)" [linux arch/x86/kernel/fpu/xstate.c:1772-1773 @ v6.12], and the component-name table lists "AMX Tile config" and "AMX Tile data" [linux arch/x86/kernel/fpu/xstate.c:62-63 @ v6.12]. The enumerator positions agree: in enum xfeature the entries run FP=0, SSE=1, ..., XTILE_CFG=17, XTILE_DATA=18 [linux arch/x86/include/asm/fpu/types.h:104-130 @ v6.12, XFEATURE_XTILE_DATA at :127]. The exported header at v6.12 also defines ARCH_XCOMP_TILECFG 17 and ARCH_XCOMP_TILEDATA 18 [linux arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/prctl.h:19-20 @ v6.12]; those two lines are absent at v5.16 (21-line file) and absent from the local Ubuntu header. The kernel document the comment names exists at that path at v6.12 and says "AMX TILE_DATA (XSTATE component 18)"; its example program uses #define ARCH_XCOMP_TILEDATA 18, #define ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM 0x1023 (both inside #ifndef guards, the same pattern as the source) and syscall(SYS_arch_prctl, ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM, ARCH_XCOMP_TILEDATA) [linux Documentation/arch/x86/xstate.rst:9, :105-107, :125-127, :131 @ v6.12]. The master copy of that document is byte-identical to v6.12 as of 2026-09-10 [diff of the two saved copies].

Two naming nuances, neither affecting behaviour. Decision for jhan

Optional, not needed to resolve. (a) The comment at src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:16 writes the identifier as XFEATURE_XTILEDATA; no kernel source spells it that way. The kernel's enumerator is XFEATURE_XTILE_DATA (arch/x86/include/asm/fpu/types.h:127 @ v6.12) and the exported header (arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/prctl.h:20 @ v6.12) and the xstate.rst example (:106) both use ARCH_XCOMP_TILEDATA. If jhan wants a name a reader can grep for in kernel headers, ARCH_XCOMP_TILEDATA is the exported one; the value 18 is correct either way. (b) The document path the comment names, Documentation/arch/x86/xstate.rst, is valid in current kernels (v6.12 and master); at v5.16 the same document is Documentation/x86/xstate.rst. A reader on an older tree will not find the path; adding "(Documentation/x86/xstate.rst in older trees)" is a one-token fix if wanted. Neither change is proposed as a commit on the PR; both are jhan's call.

Insufficient data

  • Whether Intel's Software Developer's Manual spells the XSAVE component 'XTILEDATA' (the spelling the comment uses) - not re-verified here; source would be Intel SDM Vol. 1, the table of XSAVE-managed state components (17 XTILECFG, 18 XTILEDATA).
  • The kernel version at which Documentation/x86/ moved to Documentation/arch/x86/, and the version that added ARCH_XCOMP_TILEDATA to the exported prctl.h (present at v6.12, absent at v5.16) - not checked here; source would be git log of the kernel tree for those two files.
  • The verifier's report that Documentation/arch/x86/xstate.rst returns HTTP 404 at tag v5.16 was not re-verified here; the saved v5.16 copy of the document is at Documentation/x86/xstate.rst, which is consistent with that report.

Reply to post

Done at the current head 26a0338c3b. The comment at `src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:15-26` explains both values. `ARCH_REQ_XCOMP_PERM` (0x1023) is the request code of `arch_prctl` (the Linux system call that sets per-process CPU-architecture state); with it a process asks for permission to use the AMX (Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions) tile registers. 18 is the XSAVE component number (the index of a register group in the CPU's state-save format) of the AMX tile data. It is the same bit 18 that the probe `detect_and_request()` tests in XCR0 (the control register in which the operating system enables state components) at `amx_attn.cpp:67-70`. `available()` runs that probe once, at `amx_attn.cpp:84`. The comment names the kernel document `Documentation/arch/x86/xstate.rst` and states that the request exists in kernel 5.16 and later. The kernel's exported header `arch/x86/include/uapi/asm/prctl.h` (installed as `<asm/prctl.h>`) defines the `ARCH_*_XCOMP_*` request codes from upstream v5.16 on; the upstream v5.15 header has none, while some distribution 5.15 headers already carry them (Ubuntu 22.04's do). The request code therefore stays a `#define` wrapped in `#ifndef`, so the build does not depend on the version of the kernel headers in the toolchain. The component number is the named `constexpr xfeature_xtiledata`, used once at `amx_attn.cpp:71`.

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3937077542, on h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:487 (old head 60d66d9c04)

I know you didn't write this but what is this memory order doing here? AFAIK release on a load has absolutely no use.

What was asked

Ben asks what the memory order std::memory_order_release (a memory order is the rule for how an atomic operation is ordered against other memory operations; release is defined for stores) is doing on a load of the atomic counter last_print_bytes in log_kv_footprint, at kv_cache.hpp:487 of the old head 60d66d9c04. He notes jhan did not write the line and states that a release order on a load has no use.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

The store three lines later also uses memory_order_release, which is a valid order for a store [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:457 @ 26a0338c3b]. The two totals in the same function are updated with relaxed fetch_add [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:451-452 @ 26a0338c3b].

The memory note of 2026-09-08 adds that GCC's -Winvalid-memory-model exists but is hidden as a system-header warning, and that a lefthook regex is another possible detector that was not added [memory libstdcxx-assertions-memorder-guard.md; not re-verified in this pass]. The PR description states that without the fix the new case fails with 6 (SIGABRT, the abort signal) and the process then aborts in the first book-constructing case, and that with the fix t_llama_unit passes 28 cases and 120,057 assertions on delphi-3bda, verified with clang 19 [PR #4265 description, paragraphs 'The test' and 'Verified on delphi-3bda' (gh pr view 4265 --json body, read 2026-09-10)]. Reply to post

Agreed, a release order on a load has no meaning (a memory order is the rule for how an atomic operation is ordered against other memory operations; release is defined for stores). The C++ standard forbids it, so the old line was undefined behaviour (the precondition of `std::atomic<T>::load`, [atomics.types.operations], excludes `memory_order_release`). The line is pre-existing on `main` (it entered in 0b5bdf2c86, 2025-04-10), this PR no longer touches it, and at the current head 26a0338c3b it is `h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:454`.

The fix is merged on `main` in #4265 (merge commit 47760c7533, 2026-09-10): the load of `last_print_bytes` is now `memory_order_relaxed`, the order the two totals in that function already use, and the store keeps `memory_order_release`. Relaxed is enough because the loaded value only decides whether the "KV cache footprint" log line (KV = the attention keys and values the server caches) is printed again. #4265 also adds a regression test as the first case of `t_llama_unit`, compiled with `_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS` (the precondition checks of libstdc++, the GNU C++ standard library), because the wrong and the right order compile to the same instructions and no check on program output can fail. This branch is based on a80b102c18 and still carries the old line until it is rebased onto `main`; resolving this thread now, since the fix is merged.

8. Ben: Isn't this tron::bf16

Replied and resolved   Action: Nothing left to do  |  open on GitHub

Resolved by jhan on 2026-09-10 without a reply; the draft below is kept for the record.

Fixed at head 524c510609: the kernel interface, the packed-Q slot, the packing helper, the apply_page_tok parameter and the kernel call sites use tron::bf16; every reinterpret_cast<const uint16_t*> of the old head is gone. The table below lists old code, new code and file:line.

The comment

Ben, 2026-09-04, comment 3937239431, on h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1419 (old head 60d66d9c04)

Isn't this tron::bf16?

What was asked

Ben pointed at const uint16_t* amx_qp in apply_page_range at the old head (60d66d9c04:1419) and asked whether that should be tron::bf16, the project's 2-byte bfloat16 type. At that head the whole AMX interface took uint16_t pointers and the call sites cast q.data, K and V from bf16 to uint16_t.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

The interface and every buffer on the AMX path are typed tron::bf16 at 524c510609 [amx_attn_iface.hpp:185, :218-219, :229; self_attention.hpp:283, :1384, :1426, :1563]. No reinterpret_cast to uint16_t remains at the packer or kernel call sites, because q.data, K rows and the V base are const bf16* already [self_attention.hpp:1434-1435, :1496-1497, :1522-1524; kv_cache.hpp:1299, :1913-1916]. The header checks the two properties the tile instructions rely on: sizeof(bf16) == 2 and trivially copyable [amx_attn_iface.hpp:118-120]. uint16_t remains only inside the kernel source for the hardware-defined tile-configuration fields and an internal scratch of raw 16-bit lanes written by the AVX-512 convert [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:54, :197, :207]. The old-versus-new table below has the exact lines.

Reply to post

Yes, fixed. The kernel interface, the packed-Q slot, the helper that packs a query and the `apply_page_tok` parameter now take `tron::bf16` (the project's 2-byte bfloat16 type) instead of `uint16_t`: h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:185, :218-219, :229 and h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:283, :1384, :1426, :1563. The `reinterpret_cast<const uint16_t*>` calls at the packer and at the two kernel call sites are gone; the query, K and V pointers are `const bf16*` already (self_attention.hpp:1434-1435, :1496-1497, :1522-1524). The header checks that `bf16` is 2 bytes and trivially copyable before the tile instructions read it (amx_attn_iface.hpp:118-120). `uint16_t` remains only inside the kernel source, for the hardware-defined tile-configuration fields and an internal scratch of raw 16-bit lanes from the AVX-512 convert (src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:54, :197).

9. Ben (review body): Shape-specific name for the kernel namespace

Done in the code   Action: Answer in the re-review comment (no thread)  |  open on GitHub

The general namespace tron::amx_attn of the old head is tron::amx_attn_h128g4 at 26a0338c3b, the header's opening comment and the PR description explain the name, no reference to the old name remains, and every kernel entry point carries the _128x4 suffix; the file names, the build option and the environment variable keep the general amx prefix (a choice for jhan to confirm), and an optional one-clause expansion of h128g4 in the contract document is proposed but not made. The reply keeps one sentence on the mirror (this PR has no mirror code); Ben's amx_k_mirror request is answered in item B2. A review body has no thread, so the answer goes into the re-review comment.

The review text

Ben, 2026-09-04, review 5116532748 (CHANGES_REQUESTED, on the old head 60d66d9c04)

In general I'm a bit surprised that we are giving this such a general name given that it is really a kernel implementing one very specific attention shape. I feel future us and, especially, newcomers to the codebase, will feel this in the future and it really seems like we should be more forward-looking.

Similarly, referring the K mirror as just the "mirror" is setting us up for confusion. This state is very specifically present to serve the the 128x4 AMX attention kernel. we should at least call it amx_k_mirror or similar to make that clear to the reader. The attention kernel is already non-trivial to reason about; I am worried that if we aren't careful it will become nearly impossible for a newcomer to wade into.

I trust that the AMX kernel itself is sound and didn't look too carefully at the math itself.

What was asked

In the body of his CHANGES_REQUESTED review (2026-09-04, on the old head 60d66d9c04), Ben was surprised that the AMX attention kernels had a general name although they implement one very specific attention shape (head size 128 with 4 query heads per KV head), and asked for a forward-looking, shape-specific name so that future readers and newcomers are not misled; he did not say which name he meant, and at the old head the kernel namespace, the file names and the build switch all used the general prefix amx_attn / AMX. His second paragraph asked to call the K mirror (a second copy of the attention keys kept in the layout the tile instructions read) amx_k_mirror; that code is no longer in this PR, one sentence of this reply says so, and item B2 answers the naming request.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

At the old head the kernel namespace was the general name tron::amx_attn, in both the interface header and the kernel source [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:110, :271 @ 60d66d9c04] [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:19, :270 @ 60d66d9c04]. The old header already described the one shape in the comment above the constants ("The one attention shape these kernels serve") but the namespace name did not carry it [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:119-125 @ 60d66d9c04]. At 26a0338c3b the namespace is tron::amx_attn_h128g4 in both files [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:113, :239] [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:28, :239]. The header's opening comment states the reason: "The kernels serve one shape: head size 128 and kv_mul 4, hence the namespace name amx_attn_h128g4 and the _128x4 suffix of every kernel" [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:13-15]. The PR history contains no rename commit: the rebuilt history introduces the name in its first commit 429283e43d ("attention: add row-major AMX kernels for the head-128, kv_mul-4 shape"), which adds the document, the header and the kernel source as new files (574 insertions, 0 deletions) already under the new name, so the change from tron::amx_attn is visible only by comparing 60d66d9c04 with 26a0338c3b [git -C ~/workspace/tron-amx show --stat 429283e43d] [git log -S'amx_attn_h128g4' main..HEAD lists four commits: 429283e43d, b60fd4911e, fcc78573b0, 5352e524fd].

No reference to the old name remains anywhere at 26a0338c3b [git grep -n -E 'amx_attn::|namespace tron::amx_attn\b' HEAD, excluding h128g4, returns nothing]. The new name is used in seven files: the header, the kernel source, the dispatch (13 sites) [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1309, :1311, :1325, :1339, :1406, :1422, :1425, :1473, :1476, :1487, :1512, :1513, :1590], the numerics test [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:33-36, :88-89, :123-124, :145-160, :189, :225-228], the dispatch test's fakes [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:5, :17, :53, :166, :190, :195], a comment in the test build file [t/CMakeLists.txt:589] and the contract document [doc/amx_software_attention.md:33]. The dispatch comment names the shape in words, "head-128 / kv_mul-4 shape" [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1301]. The old head had 15 uses of amx_attn:: in the dispatch [git show 60d66d9c04:h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp | grep -c 'amx_attn::'].

The kernel entry points carry the _128x4 suffix at both heads. The head has three: pack_q_group_128x4 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:185], qk_rowmajor_128x4 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:218], weights_times_v_128x4 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:229]. The old head had five, including a second packer and the mirror QK kernel: pack_q_group_128x4, qk_canonical_128x4, weights_times_v_128x4, pack_q_rows_128x4, qk_mirror_128x4 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:200, :206, :217, :256, :263 @ 60d66d9c04]. The one QK kernel at the head is named for the K layout it reads, row-major, one 128-dim bf16 row per token [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:191-192, :216-217].

The shape is spelled out in the named constants PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128 and GROUP_HEADS_4 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:135-137]; they existed at the old head too [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:123-125 @ 60d66d9c04]. The function shape_ok, labelled "The shape half of the gate", compares the model's head size and kv_mul against exactly those two constants [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:144-149]. The gate the dispatch evaluates is eligible, labelled "The gate the dispatch evaluates: shape and scalar must both hold", which is shape_ok combined with query_scalar_ok (Q stored as bf16) [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:160-164]. The dispatch calls eligible, not shape_ok [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1309, :1473, :1590].

Names that keep the general amx prefix at 26a0338c3b: the two kernel file paths h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp and src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp, unchanged from the old head [git -C ~/workspace/tron-amx ls-tree -r --name-only HEAD | grep -i amx; the same for 60d66d9c04]; the CMake option TRON_AMX_DISPATCH [CMakeLists.txt:47] [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:190-194]; the environment variable TRON_AMX_DISABLE [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:61]; the document doc/amx_software_attention.md; the tests t/t_amx_numerics.cpp and t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp; and the dispatch helpers amx_qpack_slot, packed_amx_query, is_dense_amx_page and apply_dense_amx_page [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1324, :1417, :1440, :1464]. These name the AMX attention path as a whole (the switch, the build rule, the document, the dispatch), not one kernel shape; a kernel for a second shape would be a second namespace in the same files behind the same switch. Whether the two kernel file names should also carry the shape is a decision for jhan; about seven files reference the two paths [git grep -l for amx_attn.cpp / amx_attn_iface at HEAD].

The PR description's "Words used here" list explains the name under its kv_mul bullet ("the kernels serve kv_mul 4 with head size 128, hence the namespace amx_attn_h128g4 and the _128x4 suffix") [pr3879-body-current.md:15]. The contract document names the one shape in words [doc/amx_software_attention.md:6-7] and writes amx_attn_h128g4::available() [doc/amx_software_attention.md:33] but nowhere expands h128g4; the CMake option text sends readers to that document first [CMakeLists.txt:47 "see doc/amx_software_attention.md"]. A one-clause expansion in the document is proposed below, not made.

On the second paragraph of Ben's body: the PR diff against main contains zero lines matching "mirror" [git -C ~/workspace/tron-amx diff main...HEAD | grep -c -i mirror = 0], where the old head's diff had 479 [git diff a80b102c18 60d66d9c04 | grep -c -i mirror]. The K mirror lives only in the parked local draft c64b0ca102 ("K mirror arena for the AMX attention kernels (reference draft; not for review)", branch jhan-amx-k-mirror), whose parent is fcc78573b0, ten commits behind the current head and not an ancestor of it [git log -1 --format='%h %p' c64b0ca102; git log --oneline fcc78573b0..HEAD = 10 commits]. That draft keeps names such as k_mirror_data (15 diff lines), amx_mirror_eligible (52) and scatter_k_mirror (11) and contains zero occurrences of amx_k_mirror [git diff c64b0ca102^ c64b0ca102 | grep -c <name>]. The thread map posted on 2026-09-08 records the namespace change and defers the amx_k_mirror request to that draft; its line cite amx_attn_iface.hpp:115 is stale, the namespace line is :113 at the head [thread-map-posted-20260908.md, row 10]. A review body has no thread, so nothing can be resolved; the review decision is still CHANGES_REQUESTED on the old head [facts.md, first bullet].

Proposed code change

Optional, not made, for jhan to accept or decline: (1) one clause in doc/amx_software_attention.md, at :6 ("one attention shape: head size 128 with kv_mul 4") or at :33 (the first use of amx_attn_h128g4 in the document), expanding the name, e.g. "(h128g4 in the namespace name: h128 = head size 128, g4 = 4 query heads per KV head)"; one line, one file, no code; motivated by Ben's newcomer concern and by CMakeLists.txt:47 sending readers to this document first. Decision for jhan

(1) Where to post the answer, since a review body has no thread: the reply is written as part of the re-review request; a PR comment now is the alternative. (2) Whether to keep the two kernel file names general (amx_attn_iface.hpp, amx_attn.cpp) or rename them to carry h128g4; the reply as drafted keeps them and offers the rename. (3) Whether to add the one-clause h128g4 expansion to doc/amx_software_attention.md (:6 or :33). (4) The mirror naming request (amx_k_mirror) is left to item B2; this reply only states that the PR has no mirror code.

Reply to post

Agreed on the name: the kernel namespace is now `tron::amx_attn_h128g4` (h128 = head size 128, g4 = 4 query heads per KV head, the one attention shape these kernels serve), in place of the general `tron::amx_attn` of the head you reviewed, 60d66d9c04 (`h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:113`, `src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:28`). No reference to the old name remains. The name is explained in the header's opening comment (`amx_attn_iface.hpp:13-15`) and in the PR description's "Words used here" list. The three kernel entry points keep the `_128x4` suffix: `pack_q_group_128x4`, `qk_rowmajor_128x4` and `weights_times_v_128x4` (`amx_attn_iface.hpp:185`, `:218`, `:229`). The file names (`amx_attn_iface.hpp`, `amx_attn.cpp`), the build option `TRON_AMX_DISPATCH` (`CMakeLists.txt:47`) and the environment variable `TRON_AMX_DISABLE` (`amx_attn.cpp:61`) keep the general `amx` prefix, because they cover the AMX attention path as a whole rather than one kernel shape. If you want the shape in the two kernel file names as well, that is a small rename I have not made. This PR contains no mirror code (zero occurrences of "mirror" in the diff against main), so your second point, the mirror name, does not apply to this PR. A review body has no thread to resolve, so this answer is part of the re-review request.

10. Ben (review body): Call the K mirror amx_k_mirror; kernel math not reviewed

No longer in this PR   Action: Answer in the re-review comment (no thread)  |  open on GitHub

The K mirror code is no longer in this PR (the diff of 26a0338c3b against the merge base a80b102c18 has zero lines containing "mirror"), so there is nothing to rename here; the request is recorded for the parked PR 2 draft and applies only if that draft is promoted to a PR (the identifier is jhan's decision, stance unchanged from thread-map row 10); the draft is 10 PR 1 commits behind the head and must be rebased before any rename; the pointer to the math evidence is drafted as a reply.

The review text

Ben, 2026-09-04, review 5116532748 (CHANGES_REQUESTED, on the old head 60d66d9c04)

In general I'm a bit surprised that we are giving this such a general name given that it is really a kernel implementing one very specific attention shape. I feel future us and, especially, newcomers to the codebase, will feel this in the future and it really seems like we should be more forward-looking.

Similarly, referring the K mirror as just the "mirror" is setting us up for confusion. This state is very specifically present to serve the the 128x4 AMX attention kernel. we should at least call it amx_k_mirror or similar to make that clear to the reader. The attention kernel is already non-trivial to reason about; I am worried that if we aren't careful it will become nearly impossible for a newcomer to wade into.

I trust that the AMX kernel itself is sound and didn't look too carefully at the math itself.

What was asked

In the second paragraph of his review summary Ben says that calling the K mirror (a second copy of the attention keys K, stored in the layout the AMX kernel reads directly) just "the mirror" invites confusion, because that state exists only to serve the 128x4 AMX attention kernel (head size 128, 4 query heads per KV head); he asks for a name such as amx_k_mirror, and he worries that the kernel will otherwise become too hard for a newcomer to enter. In the third paragraph he says he trusts that the AMX kernel is sound and did not look closely at the math, which means the re-review request should tell him where the evidence for the math is.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

The K mirror is not part of this PR any more. Context for the first paragraph of the same review (a separate item, B1): the kernel namespace was renamed from tron::amx_attn [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:110 @ 60d66d9c04] to tron::amx_attn_h128g4 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:113, :239; src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:28], with the reason stated in the header comment [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:13-15].

Entry points for a newcomer, which answers the worry at the end of Ben's second paragraph: the header's "Words used here" paragraph defines AMX, AVX-512, the dotter, QK, PV, bf16, the 64-token KV page, GQA and kv_mul, and names the one served shape [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:7-15]; Note [AMX attention dispatch] begins at :17 [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:17-18]; the document's "What it is" section states the shape, the dense-page condition and the fallback in plain words [doc/amx_software_attention.md:3-13]. The document has no separate glossary section; its sections are What it is (:3), Build configuration (:20), Runtime disable contract (:30), Continuous integration (:53) and How to verify on an AMX host (:67) [grep -n '^#' doc/amx_software_attention.md].

Evidence for the math that Ben did not check. The header states the contract with the caller's softmax update as a data-flow diagram [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:54-66] and the numerics contract: both paths multiply bf16 inputs and add the products in fp32, and for finite non-subnormal inputs the outputs differ for exactly two reasons, the bf16 rounding of the PV weights (AVX truncates, AMX rounds to nearest even with VCVTNE2PS2BF16) and the fp32 add order [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:73-81]. The rounding happens at one intrinsic call in the kernel [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:204]. The document repeats the two causes and names the test that pins them [doc/amx_software_attention.md:47-51].

t_amx_numerics has four test cases [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:83, :112, :144, :187-188]. Case 1 checks the eligibility gate at compile time with 12 static assertions: bf16 executors pass, float and fp16 executors fail, head size 64 or 2 query heads per KV head fail [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:90-109]. Case 2 checks the query packer position by position on distinct bit patterns, so a wrong index cannot pass by coincidence, and it runs on any host because the packer uses no AMX instruction [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:114-117, :132, :135]. Case 3 compares the AMX QK scores with the existing AVX dotter for 4 seeds x 4 heads x 64 tokens, within a tolerance of 1e-4 of the absolute-product mass plus 1e-6, which is the room the add order needs [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:151, :162, :165, :180-181]. Case 4 checks every PV output element twice, against an exact round-to-nearest-even reference within the fp32 add-order slack and against the AVX truncated-weight reference within the exact per-element truncation-versus-rounding envelope, and it requires that envelope to be non-trivial [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:245, :249, :252, :257]. The two kernel cases run tile instructions only when available() is true and otherwise print a warning and return [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:145-148, :189-192].

The assertion count derived from the loop bounds is 12 + 2048 + 1024 + 1025 = 4109 [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:90-109, :125-137, :151-181, :231-257]. The run of this head on delphi-3bda printed "All tests passed (4109 assertions in 4 test cases)" with DISPATCH=ON, and the log has no "AMX unavailable" line, so the QK and PV cases did execute tile instructions (a skipped case contributes zero assertions) [~/workspace/intel-AMX/exec/logs/split-gate-ntiles.txt:1-6; split-gate-ntiles.log has 0 matches for "AMX unavailable"]. The CMake CI lane's Build Tron step configures with -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON, but that step is conditional on build_required == 'true', so a cached build artifact skips the compile [.github/workflows/cmake-single-platform.yml:362, :367; README.ci.md:511-514]. CI does have AMX-capable runners: README.ci.md:369-371 lists three Granite Rapids hosts (delphi-17cf 6960P, delphi-3bd6 6962P, delphi-3bda 6962P), and the 2026-09-09 run 34375107972 on this head placed its test-host job on runner delphi-3af6-0, whose log shows an Intel Xeon 6962P with the amx_bf16, amx_tile and amx_int8 flags, and both AMX tests passed there [README.ci.md:369-371 @ 26a0338c3b; scratchpad/ci-34375107972.log:6397 (runner name), :6473 (model name), :6483 (flags), :7131 (t_amx_dispatch_dtype passed), :7147 (t_amx_numerics passed)]. delphi-3af6 is not in README.ci.md's runner inventory. What the workflow does not do is require the test job to land on an AMX runner or keep the test output, so a green run alone does not prove the kernel cases executed; the document says to record the runner and require one real-AMX execution per candidate tip [doc/amx_software_attention.md:58-64], and the PR body says issue #3997 tracks that requirement [pr3879-body-current.md line 22]. An earlier draft claimed that CI lacks an AMX-capable runner; that claim was wrong and is withdrawn.

The statement that "any other change (wrong index, dropped token, lower precision) fails" is the header's and the test's own claim [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:82-84; t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:8-11], not an independent result. One scratchpad check supports it for the weakest case: a program that reproduces case 4's input generator (seed 31) and asks which single token a kernel could drop while staying inside check (1)'s slack finds none of the 64; the smallest margin is 6.56 times the slack, at token 22 [scratchpad/pv_drop_check.cpp, run output]. This check reproduces the test's inputs in plain C++; it does not run the kernel.

End-to-end evidence: the nightly MMLU Pro run on qwen-3-4b tp4 moved +1.5 points with AMX on, less than the 1.7-point spread between two AMX-off runs, on pre-split binaries of 2026-09-04 [doc/amx_software_attention.md:88-90; pr3879-body-current.md, Evidence, nightly table]. The document says to re-measure on the current tip before quoting these numbers for this PR [doc/amx_software_attention.md:90-91]. The old PR body carried a logit-level figure, "max TVD 0.054 vs HF fp32 over 956 steps", quoted in Wade's evaluation [wade-evaluation-5544513215.md line 29]; the current body and the document contain no TVD or logit figure [grep -ciE 'TVD|logit' on both = 0]. No output-quality measurement (MMLU Pro or a logit comparison) exists for binaries built from 26a0338c3b.

Reply to post

The K mirror (a second copy of the attention keys K, stored in the layout the AMX tile instructions read directly; AMX is Intel Advanced Matrix Extensions) is not part of this PR. The diff of 26a0338c3b against the merge base a80b102c18 (14 files, 1351 inserted lines) contains zero lines with the word "mirror"; against the same base, the pre-split head 60d66d9c04 had 479 such lines. That code is parked in a local branch as a separate draft, which has not been opened as a PR. The `amx_k_mirror` request is recorded for that draft and applies if the draft is promoted to a PR, with the exact identifier being the author's decision.

On the math: h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:73-84 states that the existing AVX-512 loop (AVX-512 is the 512-bit vector instruction set) and the AMX kernels both multiply bf16 inputs (bf16 is the 2-byte bfloat16 format) and add in fp32 (32-bit float), and that their outputs differ for exactly two reasons, the bf16 rounding of the softmax weights (AVX-512 truncates, AMX rounds to nearest even) and the order of the fp32 additions. t/t_amx_numerics.cpp checks the kernels against that contract in four cases: the compile-time eligibility check (:83), the query packer position by position (:112), the query-key scores against the AVX-512 loop for 4 seeds x 4 heads x 64 tokens (:144), and the weights-times-values output inside the rounding and add-order envelope (:187-188). The two kernel cases execute tile instructions only on a host with AMX and otherwise print a warning and return (:145-148, :189-192). At this head the test passed on delphi-3bda (an Intel Xeon 6962P, a Granite Rapids CPU with AMX) with 4109 assertions in 4 cases, and that log has no skip warning, so both kernel cases ran there. The Evidence section of the description holds the end-to-end runs: the nightly system tests run one binary twice, with and without the kill switch (TRON_AMX_DISABLE=1, which sends every page to the AVX-512 loop), and MMLU Pro accuracy (a multiple-choice knowledge benchmark) on qwen-3-4b moved +1.5 points with AMX on, less than the 1.7-point spread between two AMX-off runs. Those nightly binaries predate the split, and doc/amx_software_attention.md:90-91 says to re-measure on the current tip before quoting them for this PR.

What was asked

Wade posted a Claude-generated evaluation of the old head 60d66d9c04 (18 files) with three "Blocking" items. (1) No CI run had happened on that head since bbb55ae5f4 (2026-08-25), 26 commits that changed code compiled in every build (a scheduler call in full.hpp, the log_kv_footprint signature, kv_block_planes, book constructors, the FUSE manifest count), and the PR carried neither the "Run CI" nor the "Skip benchmarks" label. (2) The PR broke the repository test policy (AGENTS.md: a test that does not execute in CI must not be committed), because four of the five new AMX tests reduced to a SUCCEED stub (a test case that passes without checking anything) in every CI lane and t_amx_logit_ab was DISABLED, so he asked for a CMake lane configured with both AMX options ON (issue #3997 option 1) in this PR rather than later. (3) The K mirror arena (a second copy of the attention keys, kept in ordinary RAM outside the hugepage pool) was ungoverned memory sized at half the KV pool and allocated with a no-throw new, which is no guard under Linux overcommit (memory is committed at first write, not at allocation), so he asked to refuse the arena when MemAvailable (the kernel's free-memory estimate in /proc/meminfo) is below its size plus a margin, or to pre-touch its pages (write each page once at allocation so the failure happens there).

CI therefore has AMX-capable runners beyond the three listed; what it does not do is require the test job to land on one or keep the test output [facts.md, Corrections section; doc/amx_software_attention.md:58-64]. Reply to post

This evaluation looked at the pre-split head 60d66d9c04. The PR was split on 2026-09-08; this PR now holds only the canonical AMX path. The three blocking points stand as follows.

**CI run and labels.** The PR is not a draft, so `Run CI` does not apply. It carries `Skip benchmarks`, which every PR gets and which skips only the two benchmark jobs. The "CMake and CTest" workflow ran and passed on this code before its last four commits, and both AMX tests executed in it. Those four commits change one header only: comments, where the packed queries are stored, a compile-time size check, a scope guard (an object whose destructor releases the AMX tile configuration on every exit from the function), and a dropped default argument; none changes what is computed. Of the every-build changes you listed, only the renamed KV-block planes constant is still here; the memory-order fix landed as #4265 (merged), and the rest left with the K-mirror code.

**Test policy.** The CMake CI lane now builds with the AMX option on, so the kernels, the dispatch and both remaining tests (`t_amx_numerics`, `t_amx_dispatch_dtype`) compile on every run; no test in this PR reduces to a SUCCEED stub in CI any more. The mirror and arena tests moved to the K-mirror draft, and `t_amx_logit_ab` was a tool, not a test. One gap remains, tracked in #3997: the kernel cases of `t_amx_numerics` run real AMX instructions only when the test job lands on an AMX host, and CI neither pins the job to one nor keeps the test output. The proof for this head is a run on delphi-3bda, where `t_amx_numerics` passed with both kernel cases executing.

**Mirror arena.** Not in this PR. The guard you describe (refuse the arena when free memory is short, or touch the pages at allocation time) is an open item for the K-mirror draft, which has not been opened as a PR.

**Your "verified correct" bullets.** The dense predicate is now a named function with the same conditions; the mirror-coherence bullet does not apply here; the kernels kept their statements through the renames, and `t_amx_numerics` checks the packer layout bit for bit. The fake-kernel link order holds by construction: the test's own objects are linked before the static tron library, so the real kernel object is never pulled in.

12. Wade (PR comment): Evaluation, "Should fix" items

Done in the code   Action: Answer in the re-review comment (no thread)  |  open on GitHub

DONE for items 1 and 5 (decided 2026-09-11, applied the same day): commit 4290402491 on jhan-amx-p0 (pushed 2026-09-11 02:32 UTC) adds the static_assert on the s_pages layout [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1497-1502] and a tron::finally scope guard that holds the end_region() call [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1350-1354], replacing the explicit end_region() block that was at :1414-1416 of 524c510609. Tests on delphi-3bda: option ON at 4290402491, t_amx_numerics 4109 assertions / 4 cases, t_amx_dispatch_dtype 1559 / 1, t_llama_unit 120054 / 27, all passed [exec/logs/split-w3edits2-on.txt:5-7]; option OFF at 0265056ccb (the first version of the commit; its only difference from 4290402491 is the lambda capture [amx_on] -> [&] on a line inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, so the option-OFF source is the same), t_llama_unit 120054 / 27 passed [exec/logs/split-w3edits-off.txt:5]. Items 2 and 4 concern code that left this PR in the split. Item 3 (a rounding-difference sentence for the PR description) is still jhan's call. The item has no comment thread; the reply below goes out as a PR-level comment once jhan says so.

Summary of our responses

  1. s_pages layout: now asserted at compile time (4290402491); layout was already correct.
  2. FUSE assert: gauge left with the K-mirror draft; clamp-and-log proposed there.
  3. Numerics claim: TVD sentence removed; rounding difference documented and tested; description addition proposed.
  4. Page-share counters: now PR #4267, merged; the weekly coverage lane compiles them.
  5. Tile-region unwind: scope guard added (4290402491); no path skipped the release before.

Wade posted a Claude evaluation of the old head 60d66d9c04 as a PR comment on 2026-09-04 (comment 5544513215). The full text is quoted once in the appendix; the items handled here are summarised in "What was asked".

What was asked

A Claude evaluation of the old head 60d66d9c04, posted as a PR-level comment, listed five "Should fix" items: (1) add a static_assert (a compile-time check) that the 4-by-64 score array s_pages is laid out as four consecutive rows of 64 floats, because the AMX kernels receive one raw pointer plus a row stride; (2) replace TRON_ASSERT(v >= 0) (an assertion that aborts the process) inside a FUSE read callback (FUSE is the file-system interface through which rinzler, the production server, exposes its statistics as files) with clamp-to-zero and a log line; (3) make the numerics claim interpretable by reporting the same-token AVX-vs-HF (Hugging Face fp32 reference) TVD (total variation distance) next to the AMX figure, and state the PV weight rounding change (truncation on the AVX path, round-to-nearest-even on the AMX path) as deliberate in the PR body. Items (4) and (5): move the page_share_counters.hpp instrumentation out of the tree, and give begin_region() (the call that loads the AMX tile configuration) an unwind path so TILERELEASE runs if the page loop exits early.

What is true at the head (4290402491)

Item 1, s_pages layout. apply_dense_amx_page declares the score array as tensor<operation_kv_mul, page::page_size> s_pages [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1496]. The AVX loop in apply_page_tok declares its own variable of the same type [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1622]. The QK kernel receives &s_pages[0][0] with row stride page::page_size floats [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1504-1505], and the PV kernel receives the same pointer and stride [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1530-1532]. The kernels index rows as s + k * s_stride_floats [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:182-183] and exp_rows + q * row_stride_floats [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:200-201]. The layout follows from the definition today. tensor<d0, d1, ...> stores its rows in a union holding alignas(chunk_alignment) std::array<element, d0> data [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:251-253]; element is tensor<d1, ...> [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:238]; operator[] returns data[i] [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:285-287]. tensor<N> derives from expr<tensor<N>, N> [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:31] and holds alignas(chunk_alignment) std::array<float, N> data as its only non-static data member [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:34]; operator[] returns data[i] [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:150-152]. The base expr<B, N> has one static constexpr member and member functions only, no non-static data members, so it adds no bytes [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:87-138]. chunk_alignment is chunk_size * sizeof(float) [h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:86-87]; chunk_size is TRON_CHUNK_SIZE, 16 when TRON_AVX512_ENABLED is defined and 8 otherwise [h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:17-21]; the tron target defines TRON_AVX512_ENABLED when the CMake variable AVX512 is set [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:241-243]; the AMX option refuses any chunk size other than 16 at compile time [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:29-35]. So in every build that can compile the AMX path one row is 256 bytes (64 floats x 4 bytes; a multiple of the 64-byte alignment, so no tail padding; computed from the definition, not measured) and the four rows are consecutive because std::array is contiguous. At 4290402491 a static_assert states this: sizeof(s_pages) == operation_kv_mul * page::page_size * sizeof(float), placed directly after the declaration under a three-line comment [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1497-1502]. The two earlier static_asserts in apply_dense_amx_page check the eligibility gate (activation scalar type plus shape) and PAGE_TOKENS_64 == page::page_size [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1484-1488]; the other static_asserts in the file are at :531, :532, :813, :815 and :1433, and the new one is the only one that mentions sizeof [grep static_assert h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp]. At the previous head 524c510609 (the head before this commit; the evaluation itself reviewed 60d66d9c04) nothing asserted the layout.

Item 2, the FUSE assert. At the old head the assertion was TRON_ASSERT(v >= 0, "k_mirror_arena_bytes negative: {}", v) inside register_k_mirror_fuse_stats(), the gauge (a statistics file whose value is computed when read) for the K mirror arena [src/rinzler.cpp:3369-3381 @ 60d66d9c04, assert at :3378]. That gauge was added by f9b678e5d0 on 2026-08-27 [git log -S'k_mirror_arena_bytes negative' 60d66d9c04 -- src/rinzler.cpp]. This PR changes 14 files and src/rinzler.cpp is not one of them [git -C ~/workspace/tron-amx diff --stat main...HEAD]. The K mirror, and with it that gauge, is the parked PR 2 draft, which keeps the same assert [src/rinzler.cpp:3968-3980, assert at :3977 @ c64b0ca102 in ~/workspace/ai-runs/tron-split; the draft's diff against 26a0338c3b adds 21 lines to src/rinzler.cpp; src/rinzler.cpp is identical at 26a0338c3b, 524c510609 and 4290402491 (git diff --stat 524c510609 4290402491 -- src/rinzler.cpp is empty), so the same holds against this head]. Two other gauges on main, cache_pinned_bytes and cache_opportunistic_bytes, follow the same assert-before-uint64-cast pattern, with a comment saying the assert is meant to surface a transition bug [src/rinzler.cpp:4118-4120, :4126, :4135 @ origin/main 815ce9253c]; they were added by 414b0073d6 on 2026-06-19 [git -C ~/workspace/tron log -1 414b0073d6]. The evaluation's point (a statistics read must not abort the server) applies to those two gauges as well; they are outside this PR. The merged counters PR #4267 touches 9 files and none is src/rinzler.cpp [git -C ~/workspace/tron show --stat 9eb8a7ef42]; publishing the counters as FUSE files is issue #4303, OPEN, created 2026-09-10 00:09 UTC [gh issue view 4303].

Item 3, numerics wording. The old description said "logit A/B vs HF fp32 references: max TVD 0.054 over 956 steps, argmax flips only at near-ties" [PR3879/new-PRs/PR1/pr-body.v0-20260827-github-presplit.md:22]. That sentence was ambiguous: the 0.054 was the AMX-vs-AVX TVD on the same teacher-forced tokens, and "vs HF fp32 references" referred to the goldens the steps were aligned against [exec/QUEUE.md:68, "AMX-vs-AVX: max TVD 0.054, median 0.0000, 1/148 over 0.05"]. The same campaign (T1, 2026-08-19, Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507, teacher forced, pre-split August build) measured both arms against the HF fp32 reference on the first prompt's 148 aligned steps and found them at identical distance: max TVD 0.7139 in both arms, outliers 117 vs 116 of 148, argmax agreement with HF 126 of 148 in both arms [exec/QUEUE.md:68, "BOTH arms vs HF: IDENTICAL distance (max TVD 0.7139 both; outliers 117 vs 116/148; argmax-vs-HF 126/148 both)"]; the median tron-vs-HF TVD was about 0.11 with AMX off [rampup/2026-08-19-amx-status.html:32; exec/results/dd/verdict.html:78]. Over three prompts and 956 aligned steps the AMX-vs-AVX max TVD stayed 0.054 with 1 step of 956 above 0.05, and both arms stayed at identical distance to HF, median 0.09 to 0.14 per prompt [exec/QUEUE.md:68, "CUMULATIVE T1 (3 prompts, 956 aligned steps)"; definitive-decode/index.html:389; amx-design.md:106]. So the same-token AVX baseline the evaluation asked for exists for the August pre-split build. The raw per-step dumps exist [exec/results/slot1/qwen-logits-off.bin and qwen-logits-on.bin, 2026-08-18; exec/results/t4/t1ext-t1-prompt2-{off,on}.bin and t1ext-t1-prompt3-{off,on}.bin, 2026-08-19]; the numbers above (0.054; 0.7139 both arms; argmax 126/148; median about 0.11; 0.09-0.14 per prompt) are report records taken from exec/QUEUE.md:68 and rampup/2026-08-19-amx-status.html:32, not re-derived from the raw dumps; the reply says "as recorded in the August report" for that reason. The dump tool was t_amx_logit_ab, a disabled ctest entry run by hand [t/t_amx_logit_ab.cpp:1-10 @ 60d66d9c04]; it is not among this PR's 14 files [git diff --stat main...HEAD]. No such run exists for 4290402491.

The current description contains no TVD figure and no HF comparison [pr3879-body-current.md, grep TVD: none]. It carries the MMLU Pro row: 66.14% with the kill switch, 67.64% with AMX, +1.5 points, smaller than the 1.7 points between two unchanged AMX-off runs [pr3879-body-current.md:70]; those binaries "were built before the rebase and before this split" [pr3879-body-current.md:63]. It mentions the rounding difference only inside the tests item: "PV within the round-to-nearest versus truncation envelope" [pr3879-body-current.md:23]. The document states the deliberate change: results are not bit-identical, the add order differs, and the AMX PV kernel rounds the softmax weights to bf16 with round to nearest even where the AVX path truncates; t_amx_numerics pins both effects to an error band [doc/amx_software_attention.md:47-51]. The test file's header names the two causes [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:6-7], defines the two roundings [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:38-41], and has the case "AMX PV stays inside the AVX weight-rounding envelope" [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:187-188]. The evaluation's remark that the change reaches non-dense pages is correct in this sense: the AMX page result is folded into the same running-softmax state that the AVX pages of the same token update, v_star at :1542/:1545, s_star at :1543/:1546, m_star at :1548 [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1533-1549]. A later campaign (dd, earlier build, prompt 2048, 64 steps) measured AMX-on vs AMX-off only, max TVD 0.0302, and did not run its HF arbiter [exec/results/dd/steps-r5.txt:2-4, :17; exec/results/dd/verdict.html:78].

Proposed code change

(a) APPLIED in 4290402491. h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp, inside apply_dense_amx_page, directly after the declaration tensor<operation_kv_mul, page::page_size> s_pages; (:1496): a three-line comment and static_assert( sizeof(s_pages) == operation_kv_mul * page::page_size * sizeof(float), "the AMX kernels read s_pages as one block: rows must be consecutive"); [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1497-1502]. +6 lines, compile-time only, no run-time effect. Sufficient on its own: a total of 1024 bytes for four rows that each hold 64 floats forces every row to be exactly 256 bytes, at offset 0 and consecutive. It passes because expr<tensor<64>, 64> has no data members (h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:87-138) and chunk_alignment is 64 bytes in any build that compiles the AMX path (self_attention.hpp:29-35). (b) APPLIED in 4290402491. h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp, apply_page_range: after the if (amx_on) { ... begin_region(); } block (:1342-1349) and before the #endif (:1355), a two-line comment and tron::finally end_amx_region([&] { if (amx_on) amx_attn_h128g4::end_region(); }); [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1350-1354]; the three lines #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH / if (amx_on) amx_attn_h128g4::end_region(); / #endif (:1414-1416 at 524c510609) are deleted, and the function now closes at :1419. Same single end_region call, now run by a destructor at function exit. (c) NOT APPLIED, still open (item 3). Not code: add this paragraph to the PR description's Short version (four sentences): "AMX-on and AMX-off outputs are not bit-identical. The AMX PV kernel rounds the softmax weights to bf16 with round to nearest even where the AVX path truncates, and the two paths add the fp32 products in a different order. This is deliberate; t_amx_numerics pins both effects to an error band, and the document states the contract. On the August pre-split build, as recorded in the August report, the AMX-vs-AVX max TVD was 0.054 over 956 teacher-forced steps, and both arms sat at the same distance from the HF fp32 reference." Applied: items 1 and 5 landed in 4290402491

Commit 4290402491 ("attention: assert the s_pages layout, guard the tile region, drop a default argument") is the head of branch jhan-amx-p0 in the read-only worktree, pushed 2026-09-11 02:32 UTC. It changes one file, h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp, +12/-4 lines [git show --stat 4290402491]. Neither hunk changes behaviour today: the size relation held before it was written down as a check, and no path skipped end_region() (the call that releases the AMX tile configuration; AMX is the CPU matrix instruction set). The tests below are therefore a regression check, not a functional one.

Item 1: the layout of the score buffer is now asserted. A three-line comment and a static_assert (a compile-time check) follow the s_pages declaration in apply_dense_amx_page [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1497-1502; the declaration is :1496]. Quoted from git show 4290402491:

+      // rows of 64 floats must be consecutive with no padding. The size check
+      // forces both: 4 x 64 x 4 bytes leaves no room for a gap.
+      static_assert(

+          "the AMX kernels read s_pages as one block: rows must be consecutive");

Reply to post

The evaluation reviewed the pre-split head 60d66d9c04. This PR is the part of that head without the K mirror (a second copy of the attention keys in the layout the AMX kernel reads), so items 2 and 4 concern code that is no longer here. The other three are answered at 4290402491.

**Item 1, `s_pages` layout.** Correct: the AMX kernels read the four score rows as one block with a fixed row stride, so they assume the rows are consecutive with no padding. `tensor<4, 64>` gives that layout by construction (each row is a bare 64-float array, the base class has no data members), but nothing asserted it. The fix is one `static_assert` that the whole tensor is exactly 4 x 64 floats in size, which forces both the row size and the absence of padding. Added in 4290402491.

**Item 2, the assert in a FUSE read callback.** That assert guarded the mirror-arena gauge, which is not in this PR (rinzler.cpp is untouched). Two existing gauges on main use the same assert-before-cast pattern, so the point applies to them as well. Proposed: clamp to zero and log, in the mirror draft if it is opened, and as a separate small change for the two main gauges.

**Item 3, the numerics claim.** The ambiguous "max TVD 0.054 vs HF fp32" sentence is gone from the description. As recorded in the August report, that 0.054 was the AMX-versus-AVX distance (the added delta you asked for), and both arms sat at the same distance from the Hugging Face reference (same max TVD, same top-token agreement, 126 of 148 steps). Those are pre-split figures; no such run exists for this tip. The deliberate change you name is stated in doc/amx_software_attention.md: the AMX PV kernel rounds the softmax weights to bf16 with round-to-nearest-even where the AVX path truncates, and the two paths add in a different order; the numerics test pins both effects. You are right that it reaches a token's whole output, because the AMX page result is folded into the same running-softmax state the AVX pages update. Proposed: add that statement to the description's Short version.

**Item 4, page_share_counters.hpp.** Not in this PR. The counters became #4267, merged on 2026-09-10, where the code sits behind a CMake option that is off by default and on in the weekly coverage lane, so that lane compiles the hooks. Publishing them as FUSE files is issue #4303.

**Item 5, `begin_region()` without an unwind path.** True for the reviewed head. No path skipped `end_region()` and kept the process alive: nothing returned or broke out between the two calls, `TRON_ASSERT` aborts, and `apply_page_range` is `noexcept`, so an exception terminates. The guard protects against a future early return. Added in 4290402491 with the existing `tron::finally` helper.

The amx_qp declaration stays outside the #ifdef block by choice (moving it would need a second #ifdef inside the call). Items 2 to 4 concern K-mirror code that left this PR; item 5's hand-verify TODO is replaced by the CI lane, with the real-AMX execution gap tracked in #3997. The three edits (item 12's static_assert and region guard, this item's default removal) landed together in 4290402491, built and tested on delphi-3bda with the option on (t_amx_numerics, t_amx_dispatch_dtype, t_llama_unit all passed) and, for the first version 0265056ccb, with the option off (t_llama_unit passed; the one line that differs between the versions is inside the #ifdef block the off build does not compile). Wade posted a Claude evaluation of the old head 60d66d9c04 as a PR comment on 2026-09-04 (comment 5544513215). The full text is quoted once in the appendix; the items handled here are summarised in "What was asked".

What was asked

Wade's PR-level comment (a Claude evaluation of the old head 60d66d9c04) listed five "Minor" items: (1) two AMX-only variables, did_amx (the flag recording whether the AMX path ran) and amx_qp (the pointer to the packed query), are declared outside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (the build option that compiles the AMX code) and passed to apply_page_tok in every build, and the default argument on apply_page_tok exists only for this one caller; (2) mirror(), the accessor of the K mirror (the second, tile-layout copy of the attention keys), asserts slot.i == 0 under the EAGLE storage view (the remap that gives the draft model's logical slot 0 its own physical slot) while kv_block() does not, and the Note should say so; (3) the amx_mirror_aware_cache concept (a C++ compile-time interface check), create_sequence_cache and the three_argument_sequence_cache test fixture are a lot of machinery for one production cache, and he would have put the bool in the concept. Items (4) and (5): t_amx_arena_leak replaces the global aligned operator new[]/delete[] (a fragile but acceptable proxy), and the TODO(jhan) in src/tron/CMakeLists.txt telling contributors to hand-verify on an AMX host before pushing is not enforceable.

Reply to post

The evaluation looked at the old head 60d66d9c04. After the split this PR holds only the canonical path (the AMX kernels, their dispatch, the build option and the tests), so the five Minor items stand as follows.

**Item 1.** `did_amx` is gone; the AMX path in `apply_page_tok` is one gated call inside the build-option block. Two remnants remained: the packed-query pointer `amx_qp` is declared outside that block and passed to `apply_page_tok` in every build, and that parameter had a `= nullptr` default although its only caller always passes it. The default is removed in 4290402491; moving the declaration inside the block would need a second `#ifdef` inside the call, so that part stays.

**Items 2 to 4.** The K mirror, the `amx_mirror_aware_cache` concept with its factory and test fixture, and `t_amx_arena_leak` are no longer in this PR; its diff has no occurrence of "mirror" or "arena". What remains in kv_cache.hpp is the named constant for the two KV-block planes and two accessors for the raw V storage. Your three notes apply to the K-mirror draft, which is unpublished, and are recorded for it.

**Item 5.** The TODO asking contributors to hand-verify on an AMX host is gone. The CMake CI lane now builds with the AMX option on, so the kernels, the dispatch and both tests compile on every run; the remaining TODO names the open item, one required real-AMX test execution, tracked in #3997. In the last green run the test-host job passed both tests: `t_amx_dispatch_dtype` runs in full everywhere (CPU fakes), while the kernel cases of `t_amx_numerics` execute AMX only when the host allows it, and the run keeps no test output to show that. On delphi-3bda (an AMX host) the test passed with both kernel cases executing.

Appendix A: Wade's evaluation, full text

Posted 2026-09-04 as comment 5544513215; it evaluates the old head 60d66d9c04 (18 files), before the split into PR 0 (#4267), PR 0b (#4265), this PR and the parked PR 2.

In the spirit of the new "PRs should never be blocked by human approval" edict :-)... below is Claude's evaluation of this PR:

● Review of PR #3879 (head 60d66d9c04428d907069ced375b93265add02db7, 18 files, +2538/−52)

Verdict: kernel and integration code is correct as far as I can trace it. I found no functional bug in the dense predicate, the tile kernels, the mirror layout, the EAGLE view remap, or the softmax update. The blocking concerns are process and operational, not numerical.

Blocking

- No CI has run on the current head. Last "CMake and CTest" run is on bbb55ae5f4f5edce49f840be8cac1460a5623b4b (2026-08-25). The 26 commits since then change code compiled in every build: create_sequence_cache and the root-node amx_mirror_eligible call in h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:1458, the log_kv_footprint signature, kv_block_planes, the new book constructors, and the FUSE manifest count. The PR has no Run CI label and no Skip benchmarks label. Needs a CI run before merge. - Violates the repository test policy. AGENTS.md now says a test that does not execute in CI must not be committed. Four of the five new tests reduce to SUCCEED("nothing to test") in every CI lane, and t_amx_logit_ab is DISABLED. The PR body accepts this and defers to #3997, but three of those tests were deliberately built to run on non-AMX hosts (t_amx_mirror is pure index math, t_amx_arena_leak link-wraps available(), t_amx_dispatch_dtype fakes every kernel). A CMake lane with both options ON would compile the whole #ifdef surface and run 3 of 4 tests on any x86 runner today. Option 1 in #3997 is a one-line change to the configure step and should land in this PR, not after it. - Mirror arena is ungoverned RAM sized at pool/2. kv_cache.hpp:1112 allocates kv_bytes/2 of ordinary heap per book, and books grow on demand from the scheduler. The memory-pressure governor only sees hugepage bytes, so a host with the pool sized to most of RAM will get OOM-killed at first touch during prefill, not a fail-soft null arena. The doc paragraph admits this. At minimum, refuse (log and leave the arena null) when MemAvailable at book creation is below the arena size plus a margin, or pre-touch the pages so failure happens at allocation instead of mid-request. A fixed "operator must provision pool/2" note is not a guard.

Should fix

- h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1569, 1661: the AMX QK and PV kernels write and read &s_pages[0][0] with row stride page_size, which assumes sizeof(tensor<64>) == 256 and that tensor<4,64> is contiguous. That holds today but nothing asserts it. Add static_assert(sizeof(s_pages) == operation_kv_mul * page::page_size * sizeof(float)). - src/rinzler.cpp:3378: TRON_ASSERT(v >= 0) inside a FUSE read callback. A bookkeeping bug in a stats gauge would abort the server. Clamp to zero and log instead. - Numerics claim is not interpretable as stated. "max TVD 0.054 vs HF fp32 over 956 steps" has no AVX-vs-HF column beside it. Report the same-token AVX baseline so the reader can see AMX's added delta rather than the existing bf16 delta. Also state the truncation-vs-RNE PV weight rounding as a deliberate numerics change in the PR body, since it changes outputs even for non-dense pages once a page becomes dense. - h/tron/kernels/page_share_counters.hpp: 91 lines of investigation instrumentation with a process-exit destructor, compiled by nobody, referencing an outer pending variable that only a -DTRON_PAGE_SHARE_COUNTERS build would check. The whole-path benchmark was moved out of the tree for the same reason. Move this to the notebook repo too. - begin_region() at self_attention.hpp:1380 has no unwind path. If anything in the page loop asserts or throws, TILERELEASE never runs. Harmless today because asserts abort, but a scope guard is two lines.

Minor

- did_amx and amx_qp are declared outside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH and passed to apply_page_tok in every build. Harmless, but the default argument on apply_page_tok exists only for this and there is one caller. - mirror() at kv_cache.hpp:1323 asserts slot.i == 0 under the EAGLE view while kv_block() does not. The mirror is stricter than the canonical accessor; fine, but say so in the Note. - amx_mirror_aware_cache concept plus create_sequence_cache plus the three_argument_sequence_cache test fixture exist to keep a bool off sequence_cache for a hypothetical second cache type. It is a lot of machinery for one production cache. Not wrong, but I would have added the bool to the concept. - t_amx_arena_leak replaces global aligned operator new[]/delete[]. It works because std::allocator uses the non-array forms, as the comment says, but it is a fragile proxy. Acceptable given it is the only way without a sanitizer lane. - The TODO(jhan) at src/tron/CMakeLists.txt telling contributors to hand-verify on an AMX host before pushing is not enforceable and will be ignored by the next person who refactors kv_cache.hpp.

Verified correct

- Dense predicate: begin == 0 excludes the EAGLE first-token case, end == count() excludes partial and stale pages, at_offset(63) < tok_hi with contiguous token ids keeps the page inside one visibility range, and checking the window on offset 0 covers all later offsets. - Mirror coherence: the only production K writer is save_k in model.hpp, mirror write precedes mark_k_complete, page moves go through copy_storage_slot, and the EAGLE view remap matches kv_block. - Kernel indexing: VNNI panel offsets, pair-interleaved V as B operand, cvtne2ps_pbh(hi, lo) lane order, 16-row tile store buffers, and the canonical transpose all check out. - Static-archive fake-kernel trick in t_amx_dispatch_dtype is sound as long as the test object precedes libtron.a on the link line.

Appendix B: how this page was produced

The open threads were fetched from GitHub on 2026-09-10 (GraphQL reviewThreads, 11 threads, 8 unresolved). For each open item one agent drafted the status and reply from the code at 26a0338c3b in the read-only checkout ~/workspace/tron-amx, one to three adversarial agents tried to refute every cited line and number (lenses: code correctness, the reviewer's own question, arithmetic), and the drafter revised. A final agent checked that every open comment, every paragraph of Ben's review body and every bullet of Wade's evaluation is covered. The per-item JSON records and the workflow transcript live in the session scratchpad. No worktree was modified and nothing was posted.

Sources the page draws on: the thread map jhan posted on 2026-09-08 (PR comment 5578693039, line numbers there are for fcc78573b0 and are stale); the 2026-08-31 measurements answering Jeremy (exec/results/jeremy-measure-20260831); the attn_accum prototype worktree ~/workspace/ai-runs/tron-qpack-accum and its build logs (exec/logs/split-qpackA.txt, split-qpackB.txt, split-qpackBoff.txt); PR #4265 and #4267 on GitHub.
